## Phase 7 — Who Your Climber Is   ✅ ALL FOUR AXES WIRED 2026-08-24

**Every climber in this game is currently identical at birth.** The 2D
game's creation flow picks an **origin**, an **archetype**, a **flaw**, a
**temperament** on four personality axes (Disciplined↔Impulsive,
Bold↔Cautious, Social↔Loner, Purist↔Pragmatist), and rolls **two hidden
talents** — one gift, one anti-talent — that surface only through play, plus
**quirks** you pick and quirks you *earn* from how you actually climb.
`Sim/` has none of it: `Climber` is five skills and a morphology.

This is the largest single unrecorded gap and the cheapest to close, because
every consumer already exists. The session resolver, the day loop, the
factions and the sponsor all read a `Climber`; personality bends odds the
same way conditions do. It is a set of modifiers on paths that are already
built and already measured.

It is also the first thing a new player meets, which matters more than usual
now that Early Access is the shipping plan.

**Done when:**
1. Two climbers created from different choices play **measurably**
   differently across a season — sends, injuries and standing all move, by
   more than seed noise, on identical days.
2. A hidden talent can be discovered by playing without ever being told it
   was rolled, and the discovery is legible when it lands.
3. A player can say who their climber is in one sentence that isn't a stat
   line.
4. Old saves migrate: a climber with no origin gets a defensible default and
   plays exactly as it did before.

**Built and measured 2026-08-23** (`notes/phase7-who-you-are.md`).
**Gate 1 passes on grade and not on sends**: paired over 24 seeds a
Boulderer out-grades a Rope Gun **24/0/0**, 5.99 against 4.85, while their
average of five skills is identical to two decimals — exactly what a
zero-sum archetype table should produce. Sends are the wrong metric and the
note says why: unpaired, one build scores 0 to 25 across twelve seeds, so
seed noise swamps every build difference. **Gate 2 passes** — a talent
surfaces from forty sessions in its lane and is announced once, and a gift
in a lane you never train stays hidden for good. **Gate 4 passes** — SAVE
v21, and a v20 career migrates to *unbuilt*, which is exactly neutral, so it
loads with the numbers it was measured with. **Gate 3 is a play gate and is
outstanding.**

**Three of the four personality axes are wired.** `discipline` bends what a
session teaches you, `purism` what a shift pays, and `boldness` what you
will commit to above the last piece — worth **+10.4% sends at your limit on
unpadded ground** and nothing at all to a boulderer who owns pads, because
crash pads price fear at exactly zero. **`social` is wired as of 2026-08-24**, and it took
building the thing it reads: turnout. `LotRegulars` handed back all five
regulars every day for ninety years, so there was nothing for the axis to
bend — `WhoIsAround` is the call sheet against that cast, and the axis moves
it ±0.30, signed, so a Loner empties the Lot exactly as much as an
Influencer fills it. Measured over thirty years of sport: **The Purist is
turned away on 89 days and The Influencer on 4.5, in 8 of 8 seeds.** This
also closed the roadmap's own *"the Lot never varies"* question and the
belayer gate that had **never once bitten** — see
`notes/who-turns-up.md`. **Quirks, habits and personality drift are
**BUILT 2026-08-24** (`notes/phase7b-habits-and-quirks.md`, SAVE v34):
nine habits earned from a decaying logbook, each hardening into a permanent
quirk after two seasons, plus six picked at creation. **A habit is what you
have been doing lately and it can change; a quirk is what you turned out to
be and it does not.** Measured over three seeds and three policies, careers
become measurably different people — the gym member ends up a plastic
merchant and a morning person with a habit on all 10,950 days, the sporadic
outdoor climber holds one about one day in eight, and only the trad leader
ever becomes bold. **Personality drift is still outstanding**: the axes hold
whatever creation set them to, and drifting them off the same logbook is a
third pass.

**One finding worth carrying forward**: diminishing returns eat most of a
*rate* flaw. Gumby trains technique at half rate and lands 5% lower after a
year, where Tweaky Fingers is +48% injuries — so the risk flaws bite much
harder than the rate ones while the blurbs promise them as equals.

**Deliberately not in this phase:** race and appearance. They are creation
UI, which needs the editor, and the sim does not read them.

---

## Phase 8 — Somebody To Beat   ⚠️ BUILT 2026-08-23, ONE BALANCE CALL OPEN

§4 ports *"projecting/nemesis tracking"*, and **that is the route, not the
person** — `Sim/DirtbagDay.h`'s `nemesis` is the unsent line you have fed
the most burns, and it is built. The 2D game also has a **rival**: a named
climber born when you are, whose grade tracks yours, who ages on their own
clock, who **takes first ascents of open lines before you get to them and
keeps their name on them forever**, who gets injured and remembers how you
treated them while they were down, who can be won over into a partner, and
who is **replaced by a new one across generations** with the old ones
remembered.

The port has partners, factions, a crew and a scene, and nobody to lose to.

**Done when:**
1. The rival changes what you choose to climb — a measured career with a
   rival differs in its route selection from one without.
2. Losing an open line to them is felt: the line is gone, their name is on
   it, and the guidebook says so for the rest of the run.
3. The rival→partner arc can be completed, and declining it is a real
   choice rather than a worse one.
4. Rivals inherit across generations, and the previous ones are in the
   record.

**Built and measured 2026-08-23** (`notes/phase8-somebody-to-beat.md`).
**Gate 4 passes** — 2.44 generations in a thirty-year career, each in the
record with age, peak and what became of them, and **every career watches a
rival retire** (16 of 16). **Gate 2 passes structurally and is thin**: the
line is gone, their name is on it, the book says so and it survives a save —
but **0.44 lines lost in thirty years** means over half of all careers never
lose one, which is a tuning call with data behind it now.

~~**Gate 1 does not pass.**~~ **PASSES 2026-08-23** — the race is built. A
rival who only takes lines removes them from the world and changed nothing
about a career (1.38 first ascents either way); a **race** puts them on a
named line with a five-day clock, which makes everything else you might
climb this week a decision. Measured over 24 careers with a `racer` policy
against the same policy that ignores them: **+34% first ascents, 45× the
races won, and 76% more first ascents net.** The rival changes what you
climb, and answering is worth doing.

~~**Gate 3 does not pass.**~~ **PASSES 2026-08-23** — **C takes it, F does
not**, at the van and only at the van. Declining costs nothing at all: no
standing, no head-to-head, no line of reproach. What it changes is that they
go back to racing you, which is the point — saying no keeps the rival.

**One balance call is open and it is Evan's.** **502 races in thirty years,
and even a player who drops everything wins 5.5 of them** — a one percent
win rate. Both dials point the same way: `raceChancePerDay = 0.10` puts a
race every fifteen days, so a third of all days have one running, and
`raceDays = 5` against a crag shut for rain and skin that runs out means most
are lost before they start. **The 2D game uses the same two numbers**, so
this is not a porting error — it is what they do in a build whose weather
closes the crag more. Recorded rather than tuned; the gate measurement is a
paired comparison and would not move either way. My reading, offered and not
acted on: fewer races, longer clock.

---

## Phase 9 — The Ladder   ⚠️ ALL SEVEN PIECES BUILT 2026-08-23; UNPLAYED IN THE EDITOR

**Reinstated 2026-08-23** — Evan: *"wait comps were cut??? ok bring them back
and the olympics. that's a huge part of the game."* The pivot and its
reasoning are in `concepts/DECISION-comps-are-back.md`; §4's cut-list entry
is struck and points there.

**A climbing career in the 2D game has two arcs and the port has one.**
Outdoors it is grades, projects and first ascents, and all of that is built.
Indoors it is Local → Regional → National → the national team → the World
Cup → the Games, and none of it is. Cutting comps did not remove a feature;
it removed **the half of the career that has an ending** — which is also what
`natlPts` was for, and why the 2D zone graph gives the Olympic Village one of
only three van-only destinations.

Seven things come back, all of them scoped from the 2D source rather than
from memory:

1. **The gym comp**, and the format is the point: **five problems, seven
   attempts across the whole session, an eighty-second clock.** You spend
   attempts, not time. That is a resource-allocation minigame played with
   verbs the session sim already has — *2D minigames, 3D staging*, exactly.
   Announced 3–5 days out, $20, six hours. Nerves are one dial:
   `COMP_PRESSURE = 0.8` keeps 80% of your normal margin, and psych work buys
   back half of it.
2. **The circuit season** — five comps on firm dates 6–8 days apart, **the
   last is the finals at 1.5× points**, with a champion, a runner-up and a
   bronze. No-showing hands the rival 60 points and costs standing: the
   schedule is a commitment, like the job.
3. **Three tiers that mean three different things** — Local (yours to lose),
   Regional (a fight), National (the mountain), at 0 / 350 / 1200 national
   points, National and up running a real quals → semi → final.
4. **A field of people, not a sorted list.** Seven named climbers at stable
   grade offsets, each with **a discipline they are known for and one they
   are soft on** — a redistribution rather than a buff, so the results start
   to mean something: Kai takes the dyno problem off you every time, and you
   take the crimpy one back off him.
5. **The national ranking** — Unranked / Regional Climber / National
   Prospect / **National Team (700)** / **Olympic Hopeful (1200)** /
   World-Class, reading the best of your boulder, sport and speed ranks.
6. **The national team as a roster, not a threshold** — five teammates on the
   paper, a head coach with opinions, a **$180 taxable stipend that does not
   cover rent**, a review every season that can take it back (named at 700,
   held to 560, cut below), and **a climber you went past to get on it who
   knows.**
7. **The World Cup and the Games** — ten real venues with travel costs and an
   **international field that flies whether you do or not**, so rounds you
   skip are counted against you; then a four-year Olympic cycle,
   qualification at 1200, and a field that is the best in the world.

**Built in four passes, 2026-08-23.** Passes 1–3 are the comp, the circuit,
the ranking and the team; pass 4 is the World Cup and the Games
(`Sim/DirtbagWorldStage`, SAVE v27). The write-up is
`notes/phase9-the-comp.md`.

**The one design call, and the mistake it corrects:** *the world stage is
absolute and a domestic comp is not.* A gym comp is set at your grade plus a
tier offset because a gym comp is your peers; the World Cup is set where it
is set. Reading it the other way made **the entire top of the ladder
unwinnable at every skill level in the game** — 95 skill and 55 skill both
came thirteenth of thirteen — while every one of 76,000 checks passed,
because they were all orderings. Found by measuring, and pinned now by a
test that is absolute.

**Pass 5 closed the list**: the ranking, the rounds, and the league.

**The ranking was a ramp with no top**, and it made two of the five gates
below false. A ten-year career peaked at 14,633 points against a top tier
of 2,200, so a career could not fail to reach the Games, and a grade-6
climber parked at National tier won none of 247 comps. Three causes, one
idea: *a placing was worth the same wherever and whenever you got it.* The
curve is top-weighted now, the ranking is a **rolling twelve-month record**
rather than a lifetime total, and a placing is weighted by which room it
was in. It peaks at 1,348 and settles near 700–1,000, flat across ten years
and thirty. The selection committee sits **once a year** rather than fifty
times in ten.

**Quals → semi → final** at Regional and above: six out of quals, four out
of the semi, a four-problem final. The score does not carry and the cut is
read.

**Leagues** (`Sim/DirtbagLeague`): five dollars, ten goes, six named
regulars, eight-week blocks, and **no ranking points at all** — what you
chase is your own best score. The probe found both of its flaws in an
afternoon: the regulars were paid on a different scale from the player (65
blocks won out of 65) and the personal best saturated in a month (2 PBs in
519 nights). Both fixed and measured: 16 blocks of 64, about one PB a year.

**What is left is Editor-side.** Nobody has stood in the gym and pressed E
on a comp poster.

**Leagues come with it** as the same system's low end: a weekly gym night
with a personal best to chase rather than a ranking. Cheap, and it gives the
gym something to be between comps.

**Why it sits after Phase 8 and not before:** the ladder is entangled with
the rival throughout — season points, head-to-head record, scouting them
before a comp, the points they bank when you no-show. **Comps without
somebody to lose to are a leaderboard.**

**Done when:**
1. A comp is a **contest you can lose by spending attempts badly**, not a
   number that resolves — measured: two identical climbers with different
   attempt policies place differently, by more than form noise.
2. The three tiers measurably mean different things. The 2D game's own
   balance failure is the specific thing to avoid and it is already
   diagnosed: a field symmetric around your grade measured **5th on average,
   2.2% podiums, 0% wins, forever.** Local must be winnable.
3. A career can reach the Games and can fail to, and the difference is
   choices rather than seed.
4. Being cut from the national team costs something a stipend does not
   explain.
5. Old saves migrate: a climber with no ranking gets Unranked and plays
   exactly as before.

**Pass 1 built and measured 2026-08-23** (`notes/phase9-the-comp.md`):
**items 1, 3 and 4** — the gym comp, the three tiers, and the field of seven
— plus the national ranking *number* that everything above reads. **Gate 1
passes**: a comp is a contest you can lose by spending attempts badly, and
form makes the scoreboard different every time. **Gate 2 passes on Local**:
flashing an entire local board wins **48 of 60 and podiums 60 of 60**, which
is "yours to lose" rather than a formality — after a fix, because without a
per-tier field shift it won **16 of 60** and you could not beat a climber a
grade above you by climbing better, only by them having a bad day. **Gate 5
passes** — SAVE v24, and a v23 career migrates to unranked, which is Local.

**Pass 2 built and measured 2026-08-23** (same note): **items 2 and 5** —
the circuit season and the six named ranking tiers. Five comps on firm dates,
**the last worth half as much again**; everybody banks, not just you;
**1st takes 100 and the back of the field still takes 5**, linear in how many
people you beat, which replaced pass 1's shortcut of feeding the ranking with
prize rep (10 for a win, against tiers gating at 350 — thirty-five wins to
reach Regional). **`rep` is what the scene thinks and ranking points are what
the federation records**, and conflating them was the shortcut. Not turning
up hands the rival sixty and costs you standing. SAVE v25.

**Two bugs found wiring it, both recorded**: the night-tick forfeit fired the
day after *every* comp including ones you won, because it asked whether the
season was unfinished rather than **whether the ledger had caught up with the
calendar**; and `CloseSeason` carried a podium guard that was never the
mechanism — removing it changed nothing and failed no test, because the
season table's zero tie-break already did the work.

**Pass 3 built and measured 2026-08-23** (same note): **item 6, the national
team**. Two lines rather than one — clear **700** to be named, hold down to
**560**, which is the grace a committee gives a returning athlete and without
which a season spent hovering is a coin flip taken five times. Your teammates
are **the top of the national field**, the exact people you have chased up
the standings, each with a role read off what they are known for; the roster
rebuilds at every review so people come and go under you. **Getting the call
once never un-happens** — being cut takes the spot, not the fact. **Gate 4
passes**: being cut costs standing, the stipend, and the coach's squad, none
of which a $180 stipend explains.

**One units bug caught before it shipped**: the 2D rep values here are 16, 6
and 6 on a scale running to hundreds, and the port's standing runs −1..1
where *"0.3 is a big one"*. Unconverted that would have shifted the Scene by
sixteen on a scale where one is the whole range — the rival's
grade-for-skill bug in a different hat, available **every time a number
crosses between two systems that both call their axis "reputation"**.

**Still to build: item 7, plus quals → semi → final and leagues** — the World
Cup and the Games. Both gate on the ranking number, which is real now. Gate 3
is theirs.

**Deliberately not in this phase:** speed as a trained discipline beyond a
personal best, and the comp *venue* as a built place — the Olympic Village is
Phase 6's zone graph and a blockout, not this phase's problem.

---

## Phase 10 — The Body Keeps Score   ✅ DONE 2026-08-23

§4 ports *"injury + physio + aging"* as one line, and the port is that line:
you get hurt, you buy days back, you heal. The 2D game has the rest of it —
**an injury hides its grade until you pay to look at it**, insurance you buy
before you need it, **cortisone that works now and degrades the joint
forever**, surgery and post-op aftercare, sickness, a **dental clock that
only escalates**, prehab that lowers risk before the fact, undertreated care
when you were broke at the desk, a shrink, **scars that flare under load for
the rest of a career**, and a comeback that is staged — rest, then mobility,
then a graded return — rather than a timer running out.

This phase is the one where money buying climbing back stops being a single
purchase and becomes a decision tree, which is exactly what Phase 3's
restated gate wanted and got only narrowly.

**Built 2026-08-23** (`Sim/DirtbagMedical`, SAVE v30). Write-up:
`notes/phase10-the-body-keeps-score.md`. One sentence carries it: **an
injury hides its grade until you pay to look at it.** Three clocks — the
staged comeback that advances because you say so, the joint that carries
what you did to it, and the policy you either bought before you needed it
or did not.

**Done when:**
1. Getting hurt presents a decision with a wrong answer, not a wait. —
   **passes.** Three diagnosis tiers, and the physio's guess is *not always
   right*; then rest, physio, the shot, or the knife.
2. A career can be shortened by choices made while injured — measured across
   thirty years, against a control that always chooses well. — **passes.**
   Sends over five seeds: 23/23, 29/25, **24/5**, 25/22, 20/14, sensible
   against impatient. Never better, and at west-9 destroyed. The impatient
   climber is hurt for *fewer* days and ends with a body 2.6× as likely to
   break.
3. Insurance is a real bet: buying it is wrong often enough to hurt and
   right often enough to matter. — **passes.** $6,250 of premiums over
   thirty years against measured claims of $816 (lucky) to $8,704
   (unlucky).
4. The undertreated path exists and is reachable by being poor, not by
   choosing it. — **passes**, and it is the sharpest of the four: a career
   that *tries* to scan everything spends **$0 in thirty years uninsured**,
   because it can never afford $340 at the moment it is needed.

**Pass 2 built the rest of it** (`Sim/DirtbagAilments`, SAVE v31): the
things that are wrong with you that are not the injury. Every injury in
this game is something you did, deliberately — an injury must never be
weather — and these are the three shapes that leaves out. **Sickness is not
your fault** and is still not a die: it arrives off how you are living, so
living well is most of the answer and is not immunity. **The tooth only
ever goes one way**, which makes it the exact inverse of an injury: nothing
improves it with rest, the only answer is money, and it costs more at every
stage. **Prehab is boring, it works, and nobody does it** — a third off the
odds, never all of them. **The shrink** is the only thing that buys psyche,
and it moves where psyche drifts back to, which a rest day cannot.

**One measurement changed a system.** The tooth as first built escalated
and then never resolved, so a career that would not pay sat at an abscess
forever: **10,688 days of 10,950, and four sends instead of twenty-four.**
Every claim about it was true and the system was still wrong, because how
long you sit at the end of it was unbounded. An abscess resolves itself now,
the worst possible way — the tooth comes out — which bounds never paying to
a year of misery and a tooth rather than the rest of a career.

---

## Phase 11 — A Life Outside It   ✅ DONE 2026-08-24

The 2D game is a **life**-sim and the port is a climbing sim with bills.
Missing, all of it unrecorded: **romance** (spark → strain → breakup →
cooldown, with neglect as the mechanism), **family** and calling home,
**music** (busking → street rep → paid gigs), **reading**, **the garden at
your folks' farm**, **fishing** with a per-species log, **cooking** and a
pantry, **crafting** from scavenged materials, **grime**, **propane and
water**, **bivy spots** with their own cooldowns, **hitchhikers**, the
**dirtbag caravan** camped near the lot, and **shopkeepers, cafe regulars
and locals who remember you between visits**.

None of it is load-bearing and that is the point: it is what fills the days
a climbing career mostly consists of. It is also the bucket most improved by
Phase 6 landing first — a hobby needs somewhere to happen.

**Done when:**
1. A measured career has days that are not about climbing and they are not
   filler — they move something the player can name. ✅ **pass 1**
2. At least one non-climbing thread can end badly through neglect alone.
   ✅ **pass 1** — measured: two of five thirty-year careers lost somebody,
   on days 4,146 and 8,901, and both were among the careers that climbed
   most.
3. Somebody in the world greets you by what you did last time. ✅ **pass 2**
   — measured: a thirty-year career is greeted forty-odd times, and a
   sponsored one gets the line only a sponsored career can get.

**Phase 11 is DONE on all three gates.**

**Pass 1 is built** (`Sim/DirtbagLife.{h,cpp}`, SAVE v36). Five threads —
Someone, Home, the guitar, Books, the stove — each paying in the units of a
system that already existed: the psyche baseline, steadiness above the last
piece, money, what an hour of rest is worth, what a meal costs. One rule
under all of it: *a thread stays in your life because you keep giving it
days; stop, and it goes cold, and one of them leaves.*

The load-bearing number is `asksForHours`. Seeing somebody takes **six
hours** and everything else fits in the leftovers, which is why only Someone
can leave you — it is the only thread that competes with a session. See
`notes/phase11-a-life-outside-it.md` for the four tuning bugs the probe found
and the one open feel question.

**Pass 2 is built** (`Sim/DirtbagLocals.{h,cpp}`, SAVE v37). One person per
counter in town, each holding exactly one fact about you — the loudest thing
they have heard since they last saw you — and saying it once. The `Heard`
enum's ordering *is* the rule about what gets talked about, so adding a fact
is putting it in the right place in one list. `Away` is derived rather than
told and `Broke` never leaves the counter it happened at. Familiarity keeps
a floor, like `PartnerBond::rapport` and deliberately unlike
`Thread::warmth`. See `notes/phase11b-the-people-behind-the-counters.md`.

**Still to build, all of it content on the shape these two passes build:**
fishing, the garden, crafting from scavenged materials, grime, propane and
water, bivy spots, hitchhikers, the caravan.

**That measurement problem turned out not to be a probe problem, and is
now fixed** — see `notes/the-day-that-never-happened.md`. Hunger only ever
accrued through `PassHours`, so it counted the hours you *spent* and nothing
else: sleep at ten in the morning and the night was free, in the engine
exactly as in the probe. `SleepToNextDay` now passes the rest of the day to
`bedtimeHour`, and hunger carries across the night on
`PlayerState::hungerCarried` — without which `starvingHunger` was
**unreachable by construction** and *"the dirtbag's oldest trap"* had never
once fired in any career this repo has ever measured. After: 8,148 meals in
thirty years rather than 595, the town knows you completely rather than
barely, and **nearly every send gets said back to you** — which is what gate
3 was supposed to feel like.

**Deliberately not in this phase:** the social feed, the scene's discourse,
ad campaigns and the documentary. They are the media economy under another
name and that is a recorded cut.

---

## Phase 12 — Work Is A Craft   ✅ DONE 2026-08-23

§4 ports *"odd-jobs board, salaried-job trap"* and both are built and wired.
What it did not port is that in the 2D game **every job has a craft skill**
— setter, coach, clerk, office, guide, rescue, courier, warehouse, barista,
bench — that grows only by doing that work, plus **employer standing per
building**, a **work identity** that emerges from the choices you make on
shift, standing **courier accounts**, and **gym sets that stay up** and get
climbed by other people.

The port's work is a lever you pull for money. The 2D game's work is a
second career you are also having.

**Built 2026-08-23** (`Sim/DirtbagCraft`, SAVE v32). Write-up:
`notes/phase12-work-is-a-craft.md`. Ten trades, and three gigs were added to
the board rather than three enum entries because **a craft you cannot
practise is a stat**.

**Done when:**
1. The job you keep changes who your climber is, not just what they are
   paid. — **passes.** Same career with the teaching off and on: power +12
   to +24, endurance +15 to +17, technique +9 to +13, in the lanes those
   trades teach and nowhere else. None of it as good as climbing, which is
   load-bearing — work that trained you as well would make the salaried
   trap not a trap.
2. A shift has a decision in it whose right answer depends on the craft you
   have built. — **passes.** Something comes up on about a third of shifts.
   Somebody who can do the job pulls it off 50 times in 60; somebody who
   cannot manages under 25, and never zero. The easy answer is never wrong,
   never punished and never gets you anywhere.
3. Getting fired costs something that outlives the job. — **passes**, and
   it nearly shipped unreachable: a probe that only reached when it could
   was **never sacked once in thirty years.** Given one that always
   reaches, losing setting pushes a career into labour and it is still
   there thirty years later.

---

## Phase 13 — Trad   **BUILT 2026-08-23**

The cut ladder's last systemic rung (`concepts/DIRTBAG.md` §6, rung 3), open
since Phase 3 and deferred four times on the same correct reasoning: a
deeper game whose presentation or place is the constraint is a worse game,
not a bigger one. After Phase 6 that reasoning expires.

`Discipline` is `{Boulder, Sport, Trad}`. Sport and the runout are built and
measured, and trad is the third answer to the same question the runout
already asks — how far above the last piece are you, and did you place it
well.

**Done when:**
1. ~~A trad lead resolves differently from a sport lead on the same rock, for
   reasons a watcher can name.~~ **Passes.** About a grade on an eighteen-move
   crack (V6: sport 0.735, trad 0.453), and the reason is different rock by
   rock: on a crack you pay in pump, on a face you pay in head.
2. ~~Placing gear is a decision made under pump, not a checkbox before the
   climb.~~ **Passes.** `PlaceGear` is a verb of the live attempt beside
   `ShakeOut`; quality falls with pump at the same stance; and both extreme
   policies lose to thinking about it (sensible 0.866, solo 0.591, sew-it-up
   0.000).
3. ~~The existing runout model absorbs it rather than being duplicated.~~
   **Passes.** One `RunoutAt`, one `ExposureAt`, one `FallPenalty`. Bolt a
   trad route by hand and it is the sport route move for move, and a test
   pins the equality.

Built out of order at Evan's request while Phase 6's Editor half waits for a
PC. Measurements and the four bugs the measuring found:
`notes/phase13-trad.md`.

**Measured at career scale** (pass 2, same day): three thirty-year careers,
each run twice on the same seed. A trad career ticks about a third as much
(sends 25/17/25 → 9/8/8) and ends a bolder climber (head 61.6/66.7/62.1 →
77.6/100.0/69.9). It found the top two rungs of the rack shelf unreachable
without deciding to work for them — a dirtbag's balance sits between $125
and $290 for thirty years — and the rack count unable to matter at all.

**Still open:** a full rack never empties in a played career; head caps out
at 100 in a long one; and the honest comparison is the buttress against the
cave, which the probe cannot make because it has only ever climbed
Roadside.

---

## Past 1.0 — recorded cuts, unchanged

`concepts/DIRTBAG.md` §4's cut list stands as written **minus one entry**:
**expeditions** (El Cap tier — THE WALL's design, fold in later),
**deep-water solo**, **big-wall multi-day**, **the filmmaking/photography
economy**, **gym ownership**, **Solo mode**, and **Notown/Halloween**. The 2D
game took years to accrete these and the 3D game earns them the same way.

**~~comps and the Olympics~~ — REVERSED 2026-08-23.** Now Phase 9, with the
pivot logged in `concepts/DECISION-comps-are-back.md`. The cut list's one
stated reason — *the 2D game took years to accrete these* — is correct about
every other entry and was wrong about this one: expeditions and gym ownership
really are late accretions on a game that already worked, and **the comp
ladder is the half of a career that has an ending.** Leagues came back with
it, being the same system's low end.

---

## Changelog

- 2026-08-25 - **Nothing was listening** (`DirtbagUEPlayerController.*`, `DirtbagClimbWall.h`, `tools/check-dynamic.py`, `notes/nothing-was-listening.md`). The first PIE run of the gym batch. UHT passed, MSVC passed, a **v20 save migrated forty-five steps to v46** off Evan's own disk -- and then the creation screen came up and would not take a key. **The cause is a design mistake, not a typo.** Every numbered question in this game is asked at a counter, and every counter binds its own number keys in `OnTriggerBegin` -- when the player walks into its trigger volume. That is right for all of them but one: the four questions that build a climber are asked on the **first frame of a career**, when the player is standing nowhere at all, so nothing had bound 1-6 and the keys went to nobody. `OnChoose1` even carries a comment saying creation is asked "before anything else and without `bPlayerNear`", which is true of the handler and was no help whatsoever -- **a note about what a function does first is not a claim that the function runs**. The rule out of it: **input has to be owned by something that exists when the input is possible**, not by whatever happens to be nearby. Creation is not a counter, never was one, and borrowed a counter's precondition along with its keys. Fixed on `ADirtbagUEPlayerController`, the one listener always there, and two things about how are load-bearing. **Its bindings never consume**: those six keys already belong to every counter in the game, where the controller's own input component sorts against an actor that has called `EnableInput` is not something this repo can test, and a consuming binding that happened to sort above the gym counter would eat the levers **on the one machine that could find out**. When you cannot test an ordering, do not depend on one. And `LastCreationFrame` stamps the frame a creation key was spent on, so **one press is one answer** whichever listener the stack serves first -- returning *handled* on the second call, because letting it fall through to a counter standing behind the creation screen is the same bug in a different coat. **A second bug in the same log, quieter and older**: `Unable to bind delegate to 'OnApproachBegin'`. `AddDynamic` resolves its target **by name, at runtime, through the reflection tables**, so a target with no `UFUNCTION()` compiles clean, links clean, and is simply not there. `OnApproachEnd` had its macro and `OnApproachBegin` did not -- which means **walking away from a wall worked and walking up to one did nothing**, for as long as that has been true. That is the second silent-by-construction failure this week; the stranded `UENUM` was the first, and both were statically checkable and neither was checked. `tools/check-dynamic.py` is preflight step seventeen and takes the other end: start from the call sites, because every `AddDynamic` names a function that has to be reflected. Fifteen sites, all clean, **verified by reintroducing the exact defect** and the two neighbouring shapes it also catches. Two cosmetics off the same log: `ScriptName` on `EDirtbagVenue` and `EDirtbagVanPart`, which collide with their `F` structs under Python's prefix-stripping, and a `/** Career state */` comment that had drifted **1,966 lines** onto `EDirtbagVanPart` put back on `FDirtbagPlayerState`. preflight green on all sixteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **The screens own the keyboard** (`DirtbagGameInstance.*`, `DirtbagUEPlayerController.*`, `DirtbagDaySpot.*`, `notes/nothing-was-listening.md`). The number keys worked, the four questions got answered, and the last page came up -- *"E to get on with it"* -- and **E did nothing**, for exactly the reason the numbers had not: the E that dismisses creation lives in `ADirtbagDaySpot::OnInteract`, bound in the same `OnTriggerBegin`, and there is no spot to walk into on the first frame of a career. **Fixing the reported key and stopping was the mistake** -- the rule was already written down an hour earlier and applied to the six keys somebody had reported rather than to the screens that have the problem. So the whole family in one pass: **what counts as a screen, and what a key does on one, now live on `UDirtbagGameInstance`** -- `PressOnAScreen`, `ChooseOnAScreen`, `StepHandover`, `ChooseArrival`. The last two were on the Day Spot and **nothing in either was ever about the spot**; the spot was merely the thing that had keys bound. Both listeners call the same verbs now, so they cannot drift apart, and that -- not the extra binding -- is the fix. **Two things fell out of doing it properly rather than one key at a time.** First, **the frame guard has to be asked before "is a screen up", not after**: if the controller is served first and closes creation, the counter then asks whether a screen is up, hears no, and **interacts with itself on the same press that dismissed the screen** -- you press E to leave the creation screen and buy a bus ticket. The right question is not *is a screen up* but *has this press already been spent*, and the order of those two tests is the whole difference. Second, **a screen outranks whatever you are standing in front of, and it did not**: `OnChoose1` asked `PickABivy` and `PullGymLever` before creation, which is unreachable today only because the handover opens at the van and the van is neither a bivy nor a gym counter -- one edit from being reachable, and the wrong order for the exact reason the file's own comment already gave about the road. The screens go first now. **The set is closed**: the four full-screen screens are creation, the handover, the guidebook and the road, and the last two can only be up because you are standing at the spot that opened them, so there is no third round of this waiting. Also the fourth comment/code mismatch of the week -- `ChooseInCreation`'s doc claimed it returned false when *"the index names nothing"*, which it has never done, deliberately, for the reason stated two lines below the comment. preflight green on all sixteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **What to place, and a line held** (`notes/phase6-what-to-place.md`, `tools/check-ascii.py`). Phase 6 is the milestone and none of it can be done from a container, so this is the half that can: **the placement inventory**, written from the code so that a session at the desk is placing things rather than working out what to place. **The whole placement surface is four actor classes** and no Blueprint has to be written for any of them. Nine spots make the loop close -- sleep, bivy, two travel spots, a crag wall, a meal, a shift, a gym wall, a way home -- and everything past that is the difference between a level you can finish a career in and a level that is the game. **The travel spot's two destination properties are the one way to place something that compiles, runs and is quietly broken**: `DestinationZone` decides walk-or-drive through `NeedsTheVan`, so it is the one that costs fuel and wear and rolls for a breakdown, while `ArriveAt` only says which rock you are standing at -- and since there is no Lot venue, a spot that takes you home sets the zone and leaves the venue at `Gym`. Also stated: the five `Evening` spots are five props within a few metres of the van and that is the interface, `bWaitForWindow` wants to be **on** at a crag because the conditions model is built around a verb that waits for the rock, and the eight-page gym counter is a judgement to make at the desk in ten seconds that cannot be made from here. **And the non-ASCII finding got measured properly and a checker.** The first cut said "eighteen engine files"; measured across both trees it is **79 files of 248 carrying non-ASCII, 801 lines of it in comments, and 28 lines across 18 files inside code or string literals** -- and `Sim/` is affected too, because MSVC compiles it through the bridge TUs. Whether that matters is still one look at a route line at the desk and still Evan's, but **it does not have to matter for anything written from here**: `check-ascii.py` is preflight step fifteen, it grandfathers the 79 by a byte count so a file may lose non-ASCII and may not gain it, and it was verified by reintroduction. If the answer comes back "mangled", one pass to `--` and a `--bless` turns it into a flat ban. preflight green on all fifteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **The table on the wall** (`Sim/DirtbagGymLeague.*`, SAVE v46, `notes/the-table-on-the-wall.md`). `GYM-10` ported from the source, and with it the gym family is complete -- `GYM-1` through `GYM-12`, minus the two the source never wrote. **Not the league this port already had**: `DirtbagLeague.h` is the low end of the comp system, five dollars on a Wednesday at somebody else's gym, and you climb it. This one you do not climb at all. The source's own comment draws the line -- the existing leagues have covered Send City and The Cave since long before you owned anything and comp night is a one-off on a cooldown, and **neither is an institution**: a league is the same night every week, the same people, a table that carries, and at the end of a run somebody's name on it. **The format is the decision and it decides who wins.** A board ladder belongs to whoever trains, a handicap league to whoever improved, a team league to whoever turns up with people, one go on one route to whoever holds their nerve -- and every one of the thirteen regulars leans one way, which makes this **the first thing in the port that reads the cast `GYM-2` and `GYM-5` built for something other than a line of prose**. The two decisions can disagree: a hardcore room's cohort contains no `Social` at all, so a team league there has nobody in it who suits the format, which is not a bug but a set mix and a league night contradicting each other in public for six weeks. You are not in the standings, and the source's comment says you are -- **the third comment/code mismatch in this family this week** -- and the code is right for the reason `GYM-9`'s host does not get a scorecard. **And the measurement found a real one.** The source keys a night's score on the league's name, the week and the climber, and the week resets to one when a run ends -- so **every run of a league scores identically**: the same six nights, the same table, the same winner, forever. Measured over 251 runs across thirty years: **two different names ever went on the wall, and one of 251 runs was won by somebody the format suits.** That is precisely the failure the source records tuning its suit bonus away from -- *"only three people in the building could ever hold the trophy"* -- arriving through a door the tuning could not see, because a champion distribution measured across *different* leagues looks healthy while every run of *one* league is a rerun. With the run number in the seed: **seven different champions of seven entrants, and 66.5% of runs to somebody the format suits**, which is where the source says it wanted the tilt. Measured: 1,512 nights, 251 runs, $218,603 at the door over thirty years -- about $145 a night, which is not the point; the point is that there is a table on the wall and somebody has been turning up to it for twenty years. **Every wing column now has a reader** -- `target` and `upkeep` from pass one, `cafe` from `WhatTheWingsEarn`, `youth` from `GYM-8`, `bid` from `GYM-9` -- so nothing in that table is a dial that lies any more. preflight green on all fourteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **Which side of the clipboard** (`Sim/DirtbagGym.*` extended, SAVE v45, `notes/which-side-of-the-clipboard.md`). `GYM-9` ported from the source. For the whole game the circuit has met somewhere else -- there are three gyms in this town and the two that matter belong to somebody else, a fact `GYM-4` has modelled since Monday and never let the player do anything about. **Two decisions, different in kind.** The bid: the federation is not weighing your feelings about your building, it is weighing your walls, your equipment and your membership against the other two rooms, and those are moving targets -- the same `RivalPull` the membership drift already divides by, so a season spent building the place up is a season spent on the bid. Three gates before you can ask (forty-five members, the first rung of the equipment ladder, $2,200 of deposit and sanctioning fee), and **the deposit is gone either way while a rejection sticks until next season** -- without both halves the bid is a re-roll you buy until it lands. `GYM-12`'s wing table gets its `bid` column back, the other half of the pair cut in pass two for want of a reader. The round: **the host does not get a scorecard.** Thirty-four entries at $26 a head, nine people who came to watch and came back on Tuesday, six hours and eighteen energy, and the season's points go to somebody else. `Circuit` gained `HostedIt` beside `Forfeit` and **the difference between them is the point**: a forfeit costs you the points *and* goes on the ranking record as a result worth less than nothing, because you were not there; hosting costs the points and nothing else, because you were there all day and nobody thinks less of a host. **The bug measurement caught is the best part.** `CompIsToday` has always meant *is there a comp date today*, which until now was the same question as *is there a comp for me today* -- and the first measurement showed a career hosting **457 rounds while also entering 740 comps of a possible 780**, running the round in the morning and climbing the round it ran in the afternoon. So `RoundIsOpen` -- a date nothing has yet resolved -- and every caller that meant "is there one left for me" asks that now, the engine's own `CompIsToday()` included. Measured over thirty years and six seeds, at the two ends of the trade because no one policy can sit in the middle of it: a hoarder who hosts bids 156 seasons, wins **99**, runs **457 rounds** and takes **$404,283 against $343,200 of deposits**; a competitor who owns the same room wins the same 99 seasons and runs **nine**, because a policy that competes climbs the round every single time it can -- 740 of 749. **Bidding is worth exactly what you do with it**: +$61,000 across a career if you run them, a third of a million for nine days' work if you do not. Two things flagged rather than changed: the deposit was priced against a game whose careers are a few hundred days long and this one sees **156 circuit seasons in thirty years**, and `savings=` was a silent no-op on every policy but the hoarder -- which is why this system was unmeasurable until today, since nothing in the battery both competed and could hold $25,000, so the decision `GYM-9` is *about* had nobody who could face it. preflight green on all fourteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **There might have to be** (`Sim/DirtbagYouth.*`, SAVE v44, `notes/there-might-have-to-be.md`). `GYM-8` ported from the source. Piper's second stage has ended the same way since the floor shipped -- *"Her mother asked if the youth team has a waitlist. There is no youth team. There might have to be."* -- and the whole system is gated on having lived that arc out, because founding it first would be answering nobody. **The payoff lands one system over, and the port foretold it.** The source's version promotes a graduate into the national field; this port's field is a fixed table, so it cannot -- but `Sim/DirtbagRival.h` has carried the line *"Somebody steps up. Sometimes it is a kid from the gym"* since long before there was a gym, and now that is literally true: a graduate goes on a queue and takes the successor's seat when the generation turns over, rolled like any stranger except for the name, which you chose off a list of fourteen when they were eleven. **The numbers are re-derived, not copied**, and this is the second time that call has been forced: the source tunes per-session gains against an eighteen-day year and this port's is 365, so copied across a squad would top out in six months against a five-year age gate -- **the birthday would bind in every case**, which is exactly the bug the source's own comment records tuning away from. Ported instead is the intent in its own words, *"the CLIMBING is the gate when you don't know what you're doing and the BIRTHDAY is the gate when you do"*: solved for 852 sessions over seven years and 487 over four, and measured back out at **7.0 years green and 4.1 practised**, with a test asserting both ends. **Three things measurement found.** First, and it is the source's: `check-dials.py` reported `gainHired` read by nothing, and tracing why showed `runYouthSession` returns early when the coach is hired and **nothing anywhere else ever runs a session** -- so in the original, handing the squad over means the kids stop entirely, for $30 a day, behind a panel showing a projected gain that can never happen. The stated design is *"let it run without you"*, so here it runs, in the night tick, teaching you nothing and earning you no standing because their weekend in the van is not yours. Measured: **24 kids through in thirty years by your hours against 16 by somebody else's, leaving at 17 against 20.** Worse for the kids, better for your week, which is what it always said on the tin. Second, **the squad ran out of children**: fourteen written names against four kids graduating in clumps every four or five years means a thirty-year career needs twenty-four, so the pool emptied at halfway and the squad dwindled to nothing -- past the fourteen it now keeps filling from a longer list, and past that an initial, which is what a squad with two Sorrels in it does anyway. Third, **the queue was handing the seat to a forty-one-year-old**: a successor starts at 24 and a kid who left at 16 is that eight years later, so popping the front gave it to somebody who graduated a quarter-century ago and popping the back to a seventeen-year-old. It now takes whoever is closest to the age the seat wants and forgets anybody four years past it -- and **that fix is what makes the original sentence true**: before it every successor was a graduate, and after it two or three generations turn over in thirty years and one or two of them are kids you coached. *Sometimes* it is a kid from the gym. `GYM-12`'s wing table gets its `youth` column back, cut in pass two on the rule that a dial nothing reads is a dial that lies; the kids' area and the annex read it now, and `GymDay` takes an `otherWages` argument so a paid coach rides the gym's books like any other hire. preflight green on all fourteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **Who the gym is for** (`Sim/DirtbagGymFloor.*`, SAVE v43, `notes/who-the-gym-is-for.md`). `GYM-2` and `GYM-5` ported from the source. Two passes made the gym a business and neither of them said **who the business is for** -- the source's own comment is the argument in one line: *"The business sim is untouched -- this layer is who the business is FOR."* Thirteen people, of whom a gym meets seven: four regulars with three-stage arcs that advance when you walk the floor, and then a cohort of three. **The cohort is the good part.** It is keyed to the set mix in force the day wave one is lived out and then it is sticky -- beginner walls collect the after-work three, the one who is terrified and here anyway, and the man who fixes things; hardcore walls collect the woman with the notebook, the strong kid learning not to hurt himself, and the old guard who has opinions about the coffee bar. **Which people a gym collects is the most honest consequence a set mix has**, and this is the only place in the port where a free, reversible identity lever writes something permanent: pricing and the mix cost nothing and can be changed every morning, and this is the one thing either of them leaves behind. `GYM-5`'s second half is that a finished arc is not a finished person -- ambient lines off the day, so a week of walks reads differently with no state kept. Comp night costs $150 and an evening, needs twenty members, runs on a ten-day cooldown because scarcity is the draw, and its takings are `round(members x 0.6) x $8` -- **+$170 every ten days at forty members, about a tenth of the rent, earned by filling the room you built.** Its story is one of the regulars whose arc you actually lived, from a table rather than the source's if/else chain, which **ended on Bruno** and printed his line for every wave-two star. **Two bugs, and one of them is the source's.** The setter's line of the week claims a hundred names from two ten-word lists strided by three -- but both indices are linear in the week modulo ten, so the *pair* repeats every ten weeks and a thirty-year career sees ten route names about a hundred and fifty times each; no stride can fix that, and indexing the second word by the decade of weeks gives a real hundred. Caught by a test that asserted a hundred and got ten. The second was mine: `WhyNotACompNight` returns empty for a gym you do not own, because there is no button to explain, and `HostACompNight` gated on that empty string meaning yes -- **a gate that borrows another function's "nothing to say" for "yes" opens by accident**, and a career with no gym could throw a comp night in a building it did not have. **And the finding worth Evan's attention**: four arcs at one stage per walk and one walk a day means **the entire content of GYM-2 and GYM-5 is spent twenty-one days into owning a gym**, and every walk across the remaining ten thousand six hundred days is flavour. That is the source's shape faithfully ported, but it is the only relationship system in this port that ages on a click rather than on a clock -- `Thread::warmth` cools per day, `Local::known` needs days between, `PartnerBond::rapport` moves with days climbed -- and the fiction agrees with the clock: *"first words in three months"*, *"unhurt for a year"*. Measured, the probe pays an hour a day for ten thousand days and loses **59 climbing days** for twenty-one days of story. The obvious fix is a gap between stages, one dial; **it is a balance pivot against the source and therefore Evan's**, so the measurement is logged and nothing has been changed. Still cut: `GYM-8`, `GYM-9`, `GYM-10`, and Piper's *"There is no youth team. There might have to be"* is now in the game with nothing behind it, which is the strongest argument for what the next pass should be. preflight green on all fourteen.
- 2026-08-25 - **The gym, pass two** (`Sim/DirtbagGymTown.*` new, `Sim/DirtbagGym.*` extended, SAVE v42, `notes/the-gym-pass-two.md`). `GYM-3`, `GYM-4`, `GYM-6` and `GYM-12` ported from the source, plus `GYM-1`'s own phase 3, which together answer the complaint pass one made about itself: **every lever pulled against a static number**, so there was no way to have a bad month you did not cause. Now the target is multiplied by what the other two rooms in town are doing this month and by what time of year it is, and neither is yours to set. **The split is the load-bearing part and the source records paying to learn it**: its first cut scored your price tier, mix, equipment and campaign into a "pull" and took your share of the town from it, which double-counted the player's own choices because every one of those is *already* in the target -- so **they move the multiplier, you move the base**, and the one exception is a live campaign, which blunts them, because that is what marketing is for. Rival strength is a pure function of the day and **your gym's name**, and the name is in there for a measured reason: without it the town's history is one fixed script identical in every save, and the source measured that script as sitting against the player -- 40-year windows averaged 0.9837 and the best available was 1.0003, so **no playthrough could ever be dealt a kind town**. **One deliberate deviation, and it is the interesting one.** The source's four seasonal pulls sum to zero against equal calendar quarters; this port names its seasons off the temperature, which measures 61/121/61/122 days, and the numbers ported as written average **+0.0298** -- a silent 3% permanent bonus to every gym forever, which is exactly the bug `GYM-4`'s own comment records finding in the other direction, arriving through the back door. Re-centred to `{-0.07, +0.07, -0.17, +0.05}`, |mean| < 0.0002 over a year, with a test that asserts it directly. The argument survives intact and is the good part: **the slump is autumn** -- send season, the whole town at the crag -- and the rush is summer and winter, the two seasons this game's climate model calls off. `GYM-3` gives the two people who actually run your building a name and one true thing about them, and **the wage spread deliberately beats the quality spread at budget pricing and loses to it everywhere else**, so the right hire depends on a lever you already pulled -- checked in this port rather than taken on trust. `GYM-12`'s wings are the opposite of the equipment ladder on purpose: independent, any order, and what they move is *who* comes. **The ceiling measurement is finished**: a fully-built gym absorbs **$61,100** -- $25,000 building, $8,000 ladder, $5,500 hires, $22,600 wings -- against `notes/the-ceiling-nobody-can-spend.md`'s hoarder holding $60,378. **And the naive policy bankrupts it thirteen times in thirty years**, reaching a front-desk wage of **$2,232 a day**: the raise compounds 20% every forty days forever and nothing about the target compounds with it, so the honest life of a hire is about a year and the game never tells you where the crossover is. That is `GYM-3` working. **The probe had not compiled since the turnout commit** -- `SpendDayWith` gained a required argument and `season.cpp` was never updated -- so every measurement claim in between could not have been re-run, and preflight stayed green because `check-parity.py` reads the probe's *source* and `never-happened.py` runs a binary already on disk. **The tool's own coverage is the bug, for the fourth time**; preflight now builds the probe, and `never-happened.py`'s battery, which had no gym configuration in it at all, has both passes. preflight green on all fourteen.
- 2026-08-24 - **Where you park** (`Sim/DirtbagBivy.*`, SAVE v41, `notes/where-you-park.md`). `BIVY-1` ported from the 2D source -- *"everybody who lives like this has a map of five or six of them"* -- and the reason it was worth doing now is that it is a trade across seven axes of which **six are systems this port already had**: rest, psyche, the cold-night sickness term, fuel at the pump, grime, and a cooldown. Every one was built for something else first, and a spot table is the cheapest possible way to make somebody choose between them. **No spot wins on every axis and there is a test that says so** -- if one did the decision would not exist. The ridge is the best night's sleep in the game and costs a road; a friend's driveway is better than either and has a four-night cooldown, because a driveway is a favour and not an address; the truck stop is loud and lit and has showers, which is eight points of grime. **Two have to be earned and the reasons are the good part**: the ridge is *"the pull-off the locals do not put in the guidebook -- you had to be told about this one"*, so it wants you to be a local somewhere, and the driveway is *"somebody said any time, and meant it"*, so it wants rapport -- the system whose floor was fixed on Tuesday. **What makes the free one cost something** is two nights of grace, then a chance per night climbing to a cap, a $25 fine that accrues unpaid, and at three of them **the city clamps a boot on the van**, which does not go to the crag; clearing it is all of it or none, because the city does not do instalments. The counter **resets by going somewhere else**, which is the entire reason the other four exist: the Lot works, and then it stops working. **One unit error caught before it shipped**: the source's spots carry `fuel: 3/6/1` in 2D tank units and this port has no tank, pricing fuel per driving hour at the pump -- porting the number rather than the meaning would have been a unit error wearing a dial's clothes. The blurbs state the real answer (*"fifteen minutes up the dirt road"*), so the field is `driveHours` and the times come from the prose. **And it broke a test, correctly**: `TestBillsLandWeekly` asserted cash after a fortnight equals the bills exactly, which stopped being true the moment the default spot started attracting tickets -- the test now parks up the trailhead, which is what it was always about. Deliberately not here: **the knock** (`BIVY-2`, two in the morning and somebody at the window), whose `knock` multiplier is **not** in the table, because a dial nothing reads is a dial that lies -- the same call made about the house battery an hour earlier, and `check-dials.py` made it both times. preflight green on all thirteen.
- 2026-08-24 - **Living in the van** (`Sim/DirtbagLiving.*`, SAVE v40, `notes/living-in-the-van.md`). `LIFE-21` and `LIFE-22` ported from the 2D source: grime, water and propane, and the one rule that gives grime teeth. **The source is explicit and narrow about that rule**, in a comment on the function itself -- *"the only mechanical bite: what a room full of people is willing to give you today"*. Grime does not make you climb worse and does not cost money; it multiplies **social gains** and nothing else, three quarters when ripe and half when feral, with one the rest of the time because *lived-in is what everybody in this valley is*. **It landed on a seam that did not exist a week ago.** There are exactly three social gains in this port and all three were built in the last two days: `WhoIsAround` this morning, `PartnerBond::rapport` on Tuesday, `Local::known` yesterday. Grime multiplies all three. **A system arriving to find the thing it needs already built is the argument for porting sim-first** -- a month ago this would have had nothing to bite on and would have shipped as a number that went up. In `WhoIsAround` it discounts **what you bring** -- your rapport and your Social axis -- and leaves each person's own `showsUp` alone, because Trish being here on a Tuesday is not about you. **And it wanted the stove**: Phase 11's `Thread::Cooking` makes meals cheaper, and `EatMeal` now checks `CanCook` first, so **the discount is only real when the bottle is** and a cook with an empty bottle pays the diner's price like everybody else. **One thing was cut on the way in and the checker is what caught it.** `LIFE-22` has a house battery, and its only real consumer in the source is `POWER_PHOTO` -- the camera, which belongs to the media economy and is a recorded cut. Ported now it would drain six a night, hit zero in a fortnight, have nothing to recharge it and nothing to spend it on: **a meter that only goes down and does nothing is worse than no meter, because it looks like a system.** `check-dials.py` flagged `powerCap` as read by nothing, which is exactly what it was built for and the first live one it has caught. **And this port has no rain** -- the 2D game rinses you when it rains and this game models humidity -- so rather than invent a rain flag to make one dial fire, the rinse reads `ConditionsDials::humidityBite`, the line the game already uses for *humid enough to matter*. preflight green on all thirteen.
- 2026-08-24 - **The gym, pass one** (`Sim/DirtbagGym.*`, SAVE v39, `concepts/DECISION-gym-ownership.md`, `notes/the-gym-pass-one.md`). Evan supplied `dirtbag_v0957`, so this is **scoped from the source rather than from memory** -- and the guess it replaced was wrong. `notes/the-2d-audit.md` files gym ownership under *Coaching & mentoring*, and I had said out loud that the taxonomy therefore pointed at *"the late-career arc where you stop being the climber and become the one who makes climbers"*. Pass one is `GYM-1`: five levers -- price (free, $8/30 members against $22/8), set mix (free, leaning the same axis the other way), equipment (a cumulative capital ladder), two staff (a hire price and a daily wage), and a time-limited campaign, one at a time -- over a nightly tick where **members drift 15% of the gap toward a target**. The original's comment on that drift is *"not an instant jump; pricing takes several days to fully show its effect, the same 'meter, not switch' feel as training load elsewhere"*, and **this port already had that idiom three times** in `Thread::warmth`, `Local::known` and `PartnerBond::rapport`, so it went in without argument. Fourteen consecutive days in the red forecloses it with a standing hit, and the counter **resets the moment you are not in the red**, which is what makes a fortnight a grace period rather than a countdown. Staff are two booleans, which is not a simplification I invented -- it is what `GYM-1` shipped, the source noting that a save from before `GYM-3` "derives a plain 1.0-quality staffer on the base wage". **Measured over thirty years and six seeds**: a climbing-first career **never affords one**, which is correct and is the same sentence the medical system says; a hoarder buys on day 283 and a salaried career on day 528. Their gym balances run to **$327,808** while their cash is unchanged, which looks wrong and is not -- **checked in the source, every cost is paid from the player's wallet and nothing is ever paid from the gym's balance**: wings, leagues, the $2,200 comp bid, the youth foundation, staff, kit, ads and incident choices, all of it, with `coverGymDebt` paying *in* as the only interaction with the balance at all. **A gym does not pay you; it absorbs money by giving you a permanent stream of things worth spending on**, and the balance is how you know whether it is working. So pass one absorbs about **$41,000** against a hoarder's $60,378, and **most of the absorption lives in the parts not yet ported** -- the argument holds and wants pass two. One thing measurement corrected: the foreclosure test first hired both staff on the theory that two wages would sink a premium gym, and **a hire raises the target it is paid out of**, returning $110 a day for a $25 wage -- staffing does not sink a gym in these numbers, an empty floor at full overhead does. - 2026-08-24 - **What never happens** (`tools/never-happened.py`, `notes/what-never-happens.md`). Nearly every real bug this project has found late was **a zero in a column nobody read** -- `nobelayer` at 0 for ninety years, `starved` at 0 forever, the Cave never climbed, *"the dirtbag's oldest trap"* never once firing. Each sat in the probe's own output for weeks and each took a person noticing. This asks the question on purpose: run the probe across a battery of policies and option knobs and report every counter that is **zero in every single run**. **It is the mirror of `check-parity.py`** -- that one catches *the probe runs a rule the game never runs*, and this catches *the game has a rule and nothing ever reaches it*, which is the direction that hid all four of those. **The tool's own coverage was the bug twice, which is the failure mode of every coverage tool and worth the note**: the first run reported 27 dead counters, most of them dead because nothing in the battery had a rival, saw a physio or entered a race -- the battery was policies only, and the probe's interesting behaviour lives as much in its knobs. The second reported the entire top of the competition ladder dead -- national team, World Cup, Games -- and it is not dead: selection needs a ranking of 700, a career gets there in its twenties, and I had run ten-year careers. **A short run's output is a list of things that have not happened *yet*, which is a different question and a worse one**, so the default is thirty years. **Thirteen counters survive at full length and they split cleanly.** Four are verbs the engine offers and no measured career has ever used -- `DoPrehab`, `TakeSomethingForIt`, the dentist, and `HaveSurgery` -- which means **every balance claim this repo has made about the medical system was measured on a climber who never takes a pill, never does prehab, never gets a tooth seen to and never has surgery**: the whole treatment half of Phase 10 is not broken but *unmeasured*, exactly the state the Cave was in before somebody sent the probe there and it came back with four findings. The rest are about the game: a career gets World Cup **starts** now that the run is long enough but has **never podiumed and never reached the Games**, `gwon` is downstream of never winning a season, nobody has ever been sacked, a full rack has never emptied (a known item, and the tool rediscovering it independently is a fair argument that it works), and nobody is ever *away*, which since the day fix is simply true. Not part of preflight -- minutes rather than seconds, and a reading list rather than a pass.

Rolled per person per day on its own derived stream, so who is here cannot depend on the order anybody was asked about. **The floor and the ceiling exist because measuring demanded them.** Without them the axis did not bend the mechanic, it switched it off: over thirty years of sport the Purist was turned away on 89.6 days and the Influencer on **zero** -- not rare, never -- because the effect applies to three people *independently* and their absences multiply, so a 24-point swing per person is an 8x swing in the chance all three are out. At 0.05 and 0.95 the most gregarious climber still gets an empty Tuesday and the most solitary still has Trish turn up: Purist 89.2 days, Influencer 4.5, and the Influencer is turned away less often in **8 of 8 seeds**. **What it closed**: the belayer gate now bites -- a thirty-year sport career finds nobody to tie in with on **27.3 days** against 0 before, and 14.3 days end early because a belayer had had enough rather than because your skin had, while a boulderer still measures 0, which is correct. `social` has a reader, and Phase 7's placeholder assertion -- written so the axis would already be here the day turnout arrived -- now measures the thing rather than standing in for it. **And rapport got honest**: `SpendDayWith` ran over the whole cast on any day you climbed, so a day you climbed was a day you climbed *with everybody*; it now moves only for people who were actually there, a fix turnout made both possible and necessary. Seven defects reintroduced. **Two of the three that escaped were real test gaps and both are the same shape -- a property asked of the crowd instead of of a person**: a total across five people sits under any ceiling and over any floor whatever happens to one of them, so the floor test passed with the floor deleted; and nothing checked that one person's presence does not depend on another's, which is what a shared per-day stream would break and is the reason the stream is per person. Re-running Phase 4's career note with the argument collision fixed, its numbers did not reproduce: **ninety years gave 4 to 88 lives depending on the seed**, mean 17.4, against the note's four. The collision was not the cause -- forcing `build=1`, the archetype the broken arg had been setting, changes nothing and on two seeds makes it worse. Instrumenting which of `TimeToThinkAboutIt`'s two triggers fired made it obvious: on the worst seed **87 of 87 endings were the body, at a mean retirement age of 24.7**, and the grade trigger -- the one the phase's design is actually about -- fired **zero times**. So the measured career was offered the door on a stricter rule than the played one -- and the checker cannot see it, because both consumers call the rule and the divergence is in what they hand it. *"It cannot catch the game running the rule and ignoring the answer"* is that checker's own recorded limit, and this is the neighbouring case. The probe now matches the engine exactly. **The second cause was the probe modelling a person badly.** Its comment justified always accepting with *"a probe that declines would measure nothing"* -- but a probe that accepts at twenty-four measures nothing either, and worse, it looks like data. Retirement is an offer and never a command, and nobody quits at twenty-four over one bad year. There is now a `retire=` policy: `always` (default, so every earlier measurement reproduces) and `late`, which takes the grade offer -- it already requires forty-six -- and declines the body's until then. **With a person answering the question, every structural claim in the note survives**: 3.3 lives over ninety years against the claimed four, retirement at **55.1** against 46-52, peak **V7.61** against ~V7, and the valley ending with the player having put up about one line and the Lot the rest. What had rotted was the probe, not the finding, and it rotted because **Phase 10 gave the game a body that actually breaks, three phases after the note was written**. The commitment rule holds too: across the Lot dial the player's line count is **0.67 at every value**, which is the half the claim is about, while the Lot's moves with the dial -- the old table froze both, and that was the thirty-year scale saturating rather than a property of the rule. **One thing left as a design call rather than fixed**: a `late` climber declines the offer **856 times in ninety years**, about nine and a half times a year from the age of twenty-four. The body trigger is neither rare nor quiet, and whether `injuriesInARowToHint = 3` over ninety days wants an age floor under it -- the way the grade trigger already has one -- is Evan's. preflight green on all thirteen.

So **every skin-regen sweep also set the character build** -- `atoi("1.5")` is 1, `atoi("9.0")` is 9 -- and a non-empty build also replaces the starting cash, which makes the six-row sweep in `phase3-what-binds.md` six rows differing in three things at once. **And every `rival` run set the pads to zero**, `atoi("rival")` being 0, so Phase 8's rival career was measured on the padless season -- the exact knob whose own comment calls the pad gap "the whole mechanic". My own `-1` no-op sentinel was worse than either: `static_cast<Archetype>(-1 % 4)`, an out-of-range enum cast, written while trying to hold a dial at its default and silently reconfiguring the climber instead. That is how it surfaced -- two runs of the same measurement disagreed by a factor of two and the difference was the sentinel. **None of the thirteen checkers could see it and that is fair**: they check the sim and the engine, and this is the probe's own front door. Fixed by making it impossible: everything past the policy is now `key=value`, and a repeat, an unknown key and a bare token are all **hard errors**, because a probe that shrugs at `pads2=1` and measures the default is a probe that lies. **What survived, re-measured.** *Phase 3's gate survives and got stronger*: deleting the entire cost of being alive bought +5.4 climbing days before and buys **+0.4** now, with sends tied in 6-7 of 12 seeds either way -- money still does not buy quantity. *The day fix costs about nine days of work a year and nothing else*: work% up in **11 of 12 seeds**, and every other column inside the noise -- the apparent 34% send drop is **not real**, seven seeds are identical and widening to 18 seeds flips its sign, which is worth remembering the next time a single season's send count is asked to carry a conclusion. *"Skin was never the wall" survives and was being **understated by its own measurement***: run for the first time without the archetype changing underneath it, six times the skin budget buys **6% more burns** rather than the confounded table's 54%, costs three times the injuries and 113 more days hurt, takes 21% off the days out, and leaves the all-round grade **flat to two decimal places across the entire sweep** -- six times the skin, the same climber. *And Phase 8's rival is fine but the climber it was measured on was not*: lines lost and rival generations are identical either way, so what the phase says about the rival stands, while head was inflated 12% and sends deflated 26% by the missing pads. `phase4-career.md` ran the same broken form and is the next one to re-run. preflight green on all thirteen.

Fixed in `SleepToNextDay`, the one function both consumers pass through, at a flat ten o'clock rather than at last light -- using the light would make a winter cheaper to live through than a summer.
