The probe's own `starved` column has been printing 0 next to it the whole time, which is the tell nobody read. The missing half is that hunger has to survive the night, carried on the **career** rather than the day -- a `DayState` does not survive it, and every caller builds tomorrow with `WakeUp`, which is precisely where a carry stored on the day would have been thrown away without a word. At 0.6 the arithmetic gives the dial the shape it was always describing: bed at 45 the first night, **72 the second** and 88 the third, so one missed day costs nothing and two is trouble. What moved, over thirty years and three seeds: **595 meals to 8,148**, food from $2,692 to $35,674, how well the town knows you from 0.45 to **1.00**, sends said back to you from 3-13 to **16-25**, and *"thought you'd moved on"* from thirty-seven greetings to **none** -- because nobody has. **Climbing is unmoved** (sends 22-26, all-round 4.1-5.5, injuries, physio, quirks and the trad rack all land where they did): the food money came out of slack the career was sitting on, which is why the fix is affordable. Hungry nights stay at zero and that is the right answer rather than a remaining hole -- these careers can afford to eat, and the trap is live, tested and waiting for one that cannot. preflight green on all thirteen.
- 2026-08-24 - **The people behind the counters** (`Sim/DirtbagLocals.*`, SAVE v37, `notes/phase11b-the-people-behind-the-counters.md`). Phase 11's third gate, and the only part of the bucket that is not content: *somebody in the world greets you by what you did last time.* One person per counter in town, and two rules under all of it -- **they hold one thing, and it is the loudest thing they have heard since they last saw you; and they say it once, then go back to nodding.** The first keeps it from being a feed: a shopkeeper who lists your season at you is a changelog with a face, one who says *"heard you got the Prow"* and nothing else is a person. The second keeps it from being wallpaper, which is the same rule the injury line, the habit label and the life label already follow. **The `Heard` enum's ordering *is* the loudness rule** -- a new memory replaces the held one only if it compares greater, so putting a fact in the right place in one list is the whole of adding one, and there is no second table to disagree with it. Equal replaces, because the question a regular is answering is literally *what did you do last time*; quieter is dropped rather than queued, because a person is not a mailbox. Two entries earn being odd: **`Away` is derived rather than told** -- the only memory in the file that fires for doing nothing at all -- and **`Broke` never leaves the counter it happened at**, because the bill you could not cover is between you and whoever was standing there. Familiarity keeps a floor at a fraction of the best it ever was, like `PartnerBond::rapport` and deliberately unlike `Thread::warmth` from the pass before it, which has none: you stop being current with somebody, you do not become a stranger. **Third time this project has needed *a value that ages needs a memory, not just a slope*, and the first time it was built that way rather than found that way.** **The measurement found a bug that had eaten a career's first two hundred days**: `Seen` cleared the held memory on *every* visit, including the ones where they said nothing because you were still a stranger, so everything that happened before you were a regular was silently spent by the burritos that spent it -- `named` came out at **zero in every career** and *"thought you'd moved on"* at thirty-seven of forty greetings. It now spends only what was actually said, and decides that by asking `WhatTheySay` rather than by re-checking `nodsAt`, so the two cannot drift apart. After: a thirty-year career is greeted forty-odd times out of 595 visits, a first ascent comes back at you under the name you gave it, and a sponsored career gets the line only a sponsored career can get. **And the reintroduction pass found a test that crashed instead of failing** -- `TestTheTownIsSeededByTheNight` dereferenced `At()` without checking it, so removing the roster's seeding segfaulted the binary and **silently un-ran every test registered after it**, and one crash came back as four separate defects "not caught", three of which had perfectly good coverage. A test that cannot fail is bad; a test that crashes is worse, because it takes its neighbours with it. Sixteen defects, all sixteen caught after the fix, with three real gaps closed under the crash. **Fourth name collision the unity build has caught**, after `Clamp01`, `Record` and `kColours`: `DirtbagPartner.cpp` already had a file-local `struct Regular` for the Lot's roster, and this is the first one where the right fix was to rename the *new* thing -- both really were "regulars", so the town's became `Local`, which is the roadmap's own word and removes the ambiguity everywhere rather than hiding it behind a scope. **Phase 11 is DONE on all three gates.** preflight green on all thirteen.
- 2026-08-24 - **A life outside it, pass 1** (`Sim/DirtbagLife.*`, SAVE v36, `notes/phase11-a-life-outside-it.md`). The 2D game is a **life**-sim and the port was a climbing sim with bills: every hour in it was climbing, paying for climbing, or recovering from it. Phase 11's bucket holds fourteen pastimes, and fourteen pastimes is a menu -- what makes it a system is one rule, built here on five threads: **a thread stays in your life because you keep giving it days; stop, and it goes cold, and one of them leaves.** Someone, Home, the guitar, Books, the stove, each paying in the units of a system that already existed (the psyche baseline, steadiness above the last piece, money, what an hour of rest is worth, what a meal costs) so nothing had to be rewritten to let a life in, and **nothing here touches send odds**, for Phase 7's reason. The one shape worth writing down: this is **the exact opposite of `PartnerBond::rapport`**, fixed two commits ago to keep a floor -- and the opposite answer is right, because rapport is *what somebody knows about you* and cannot go to zero, while warmth is *whether you are in each other's lives* and has to be able to, or the thing cannot be lost and the whole phase is about a thing that can be lost. What is remembered here is `depth`, and its only job is to price the ending: half a year of turning up costs six weeks of being no good afterwards, a fortnight costs nothing, because a game that grieves everything grieves nothing. **The probe found four tuning bugs and the design survived all four.** *Nothing could ever be lost*: priced at a two-hour evening it was 1,138 evenings across thirty years and not one loss, because **the hours after the crag are dead time in this game** -- the window has gone, the shift is done -- so a thread costing an evening costs nothing. Fixed with `asksForHours`: seeing somebody takes **six hours**, the only entry in the table that competes with a session, which is why it is the only one that can leave you. *A first date was free*: taking-up cost nothing, so a career that never gave anybody a minute still had somebody 60% of the time -- meet, coast on full warmth for twenty-six days, lose them at zero depth, sit out the cooldown, about a hundred and ninety times in thirty years. `SpendTheEvening` now takes it up on the first go and charges the same hours, and the engine's second entry point was **deleted rather than fixed**, because a second way in that skips the charge is how it happened. *My own metric lied by a factor of eight*: `busked` was a cash delta and read $171,851 for a career that earned $20,646 -- off `BuskingPay` directly it is 489 sessions and about $690 a year, pocket money against $4,420 of bills. The retune was still needed: at the old rate those same sessions paid ~$51,000, making a hobby the best-paid work in the game. *And the nag line would not shut up*: it named the coolest thread, so once one hit zero it said the same words every night for the rest of the career -- a guitar two years in its case is not news, it is an inventory, and it now warns only while there is something left to lose. **Measured after, five seeds and thirty years each: two careers of five lost somebody, on days 4,146 and 8,901, both grieving the full forty-five days -- and both were among the careers that climbed most** (617 and 314 days out, against 151-259 for the three that kept everybody). That is the phase's second gate, out of the weather rather than out of a rule about the weather. Fifteen defects reintroduced and every one caught, after the pass found **a test that could not fail**: `TestAFortnightIsNotALoss` ran ninety nights of neglect and then asked whether grief was zero, and a five-day grief counts back down to zero inside ninety nights, so removing the floor entirely still passed. Criterion 3 -- somebody greets you by what you did last time -- is pass 2 and is deliberately not content. preflight green on all thirteen.

Two wrong guesses on the way there, both killed by checking rather than asserting: it is not unread, and it is not a duplicate of the zone travel model. *And the probe's rapport call read `t.daysClimbed > 0`* -- the career total, not the day -- so from the first climbing day onward **every** day counted as a day spent together, rapport pinned at 1.00 with everybody inside a fortnight and stayed there for thirty years, and the belayer's patience ended a session twice in four hundred days. SAVE v35's migration is **the only one in the file that has to read the save to write it**, bonds being a counted list. **Two things reported rather than fixed, deliberately.** Rapport still does not distinguish a career that climbed 99 days from one that climbed 2,085 -- both end at 0.45, because `rapportPerDay` reaches the ceiling in thirteen days so rapport *and its high-water mark* both saturate early. Moving it is four systems wide (`ShareBeta`, `PsycheFrom`, the crew threshold, and the campfire, which would become the only real source of rapport in the game) and is a design call about **where you get to know people, at the fire or on the rock.** And **the belayer gate has never once bitten** -- `nobelayer` is 0 across three thirty-year careers -- which is the roadmap's own *"the Lot never varies"* question with a number against it at last, and the same hole that leaves Phase 7's `social` axis unwired. preflight green on all thirteen.

- 2026-08-24 - **The harness grew a second compiler and a second standard, and found something on the first run** (`Sim/run-tests.sh`, `notes/NEXT-AT-THE-DESK.md`). A day that shipped trad, habits and the narrator also shipped **1,063 lines of engine C++ that nothing here can compile**, so the session ended by shrinking the set of things only Evan's PC can find rather than adding a fourth system. The sim half was already checked hard; the gap was that it was checked by **one compiler at one language version**, and the engine module is neither -- UE 5.8 builds at **C++20**, on MSVC. Two cheap approximations now run on every commit: the unity build compiles at **C++17 and C++20**, and again under **clang**, with `-Wshadow` added, all syntax-only so both together cost about what one -O2 build did. If clang is not installed the harness **says so out loud rather than skipping in silence**, because a check that quietly stops running is worse than one never written -- the green tick goes on meaning something it no longer means. **It found a real trap immediately, and only in the unity build**: `DirtbagLeague.cpp` had a function-local `kColours[7]` shadowing `DirtbagComp.cpp`'s file-scope one, which UBT concatenates into the same translation unit. No bug today, because a local wins and it was the one that function meant -- but **two seven-colour arrays with the same name in one translation unit, differing only in capitalisation and order** ("Red" against "yellow"), is a bug waiting for somebody to delete a `static`, after which a league night would quietly start naming its problems in the comp's register and nothing would fail. Renamed, with the reason written where the next person will read it. Neither the separate-file build nor g++ says a word about it. **Everything else came back clean**, which is the other half of the value: `-Wshadow` across all of `Sim/` is zero, no user-facing string in the three new files contains a non-ASCII character, every new `UENUM` is `uint8`, every new `USTRUCT` is declared before first use, no class has two `UFUNCTION`s of one name, and no new Blueprint verb was left private by accident. Written down in the desk list so the next build knows what is already ruled out **and what is not** -- UHT itself, MSVC, and how a `UPROPERTY` behaves at runtime, which is exactly what §1 is looking for. `NEXT-AT-THE-DESK.md` rewritten for the third time and for the third time for the same reason: **it is the only file whose job is to be current**, and it was four commits and three save versions stale.

- 2026-08-24 - **The narrator, on screen** (`SessionReadout::LastBurn`, `SayTheBeat`). The hole the narrator shipped with, closed on the same rule the habits readout used the hour before: **which surface a fact goes on is decided by how long it is true for.** *A beat is a moment*, so the wall toasts it -- `LastWord` after every `StepMove`, on one slot so the newest replaces the last rather than stacking, **coloured off the `BeatKind` rather than off the words** because that is what the enum is for and restaging a beat must never mean re-reading it, and **held for a length that scales with `weight`**, which is the one place that number does a job in the engine: the moment of an attempt stays up twice as long as a shake-out. *The last go is true until the next one*, which is most of a session, so `HowItWent` sits on the session readout beside the attempt number -- the same reasoning that put "attempt 14" there rather than in a four-second flash, because a toast would have taken it away while you were still looking at the move that did it. **Two things it replaced rather than added to.** `"shake  -14 pump"` has been on the wall since Phase 0 and is a stat line where a sentence belongs; the shake shows up in the timeline as pump going *down* across a move -- the only way it can -- so the narrator sees it without being told and says whether that was a chalk-up or the rest that gave you the route back. The wall keeps its own line for a shake that *cost* you, because that one is genuinely invisible to the timeline: pump going up across a move is indistinguishable from the move having been expensive. And the end-of-attempt toast led with `HowCloseText` and followed with the count -- both halves of that reasoning still right, and the narrator now says the sentence twice already, once loud at the moment and once persistently on the readout, so **a third telling a beat later in different words is one screen disagreeing with itself about what just happened.** It keeps the job nothing else does: the count, for the player who wants it. The send toast is untouched and duplicates nothing -- route, grade and style there, *"Onsighted it."* on the readout -- because the top-out beat deliberately does not fire live, the style being a judgement `FinishAttempt` makes. **Three things left open and named**: no sound and no camera key off `BeatKind` yet, `Beats()` is a replay door nobody walks through, and a watched bot attempt gets the readout sentence and none of the moments, because beats fire from `CommitMove` and only the live path calls it. preflight green on all thirteen.

- 2026-08-24 - **Habits and quirks, on screen** (`QuirkNews`, `DoingLabel`, `AreLabel`). The hole the habits pass shipped with and named the same day: the sim produced the line and nothing said it out loud. Three surfaces, and **which one a fact goes on is decided by how long it is true for.** *The night you became something* gets `QuirkNews`, in the same slot, colour and size as a talent surfacing -- identical treatment on purpose, because both are things you found out about yourself rather than things you won, and a career should not be able to tell them apart by how loudly the game said them. Unlike the talent, the noticing is the sim's: `HabitsDay` hands back the quirk that landed, so the probe sees the same event the player does and the engine does not have to detect a transition across a call. *The corner of the screen* gets two short labels in the same tier as the standing line, because "what the valley thinks of you" and "what you have turned into" are the same kind of fact from two sides -- **two lines and two colours rather than one**, because the rule the whole system is built on is that they are not the same thing: what you are *doing* has life in it and can stop today, and what you *are* is just there. *The handover* gets the long form under the epitaph, because a list of ascents says what a career did and this says who was doing it. **`DoingLabel` and `AreLabel` are empty when there is nothing to say**, which is the entire reason they exist beside `HowYouClimb` -- that one always answers, and the HUD's own standing rule is that a line printing "fine" every frame for a season teaches you to stop reading the one that will eventually say otherwise. One claim corrected by measuring rather than assuming: the comment said a career holds "four habits at the very most" and a gym member holds **five** -- *"dawn patrol, grinding, never warming up, indoors and climbing on nothing"*, every word true of somebody who gets on plastic at dawn and goes straight to the hard one. **The sim does not know how wide the screen is, so it does not get to decide what fits**; the HUD wraps, and a cap in the sim would have been it silently deciding a climber is less than they are. preflight green on all thirteen.

- 2026-08-24 - **The narrator: what somebody watching would say** (`Sim/DirtbagNarrator.*`). `DIRTBAG.md` §9 names the project's top risk as *"session isn't tense or legible in 3D"* and Phase 5 calls staging the craft centre; most of that needs an Editor and **this part does not.** The resolver has always returned a full per-move timeline -- the odds each move faced, the pump after it, whether it stuck, and since yesterday what was on the rope -- and **nothing anywhere turned it into words**, so a twenty-move trad pitch and a six-move boulder produced the same silence. **The narrator's job is knowing when to shut up.** A line per move is a log, not commentary; a beat is emitted only when something happened, and the whole file is built on two rules. **It reads and never recomputes** -- every beat is derived from the result the sim already produced, and a test hands it a timeline the resolver never made and it agrees with it, because a narrator with a second opinion about what happened would be a bug nobody could find. **And never a number**, swept across three disciplines by every highpoint by three odds by three pump rates, failing on a digit. Ten kinds of beat, each weighted so a HUD can take the loudest three and a camera can push in past a threshold, plus one short sentence for the career's history -- *"Off at the crux. You had been a long way above the gear for a while."* **Four things writing it found.** *It narrated every move on a route where every move was desperate*: sixteen beats on a sixteen-move pitch, because "that should not have stayed on" fired on every hard move -- and a climber fighting all the way up is not having sixteen moments, they are having one long fight. Fixed by one rule that generalised to three places: **the narrator only speaks when something beats everything before it**, so the first shocker is a beat and only a worse one is after that, and a trad leader's gear getting *worse* as they get pumped becomes the story. *The live wall and the replay disagreed about a top-out*: `LiveAttempt::partial` is an `AttemptResult` so the wall calls the same function move by move -- one implementation on purpose, because this project has twice shipped two paths that drifted -- and it drifted anyway in the one place the partial is not the finished thing, because **the style is a judgement `FinishAttempt` makes.** Read off the highpoint the wall said a bare *"Top."* while the replay of the identical climb said *"first go, no idea what was coming, and it went"*. The ending comes off `sent` now, and the asymmetry is honest: **a fall is known the instant it happens and a top-out is not known until the attempt is finished.** *A trad lead was being told in bolts* -- `RunoutText` says *bolt*, right for the HUD it was written for and wrong out loud on a lead where nobody drilled anything; one vocabulary per voice. *And the reason for the fall was the fall*: picking the loudest beat as the why produced "Off at the crux." with nothing after it, because the crux beat outranked the runout that explained the afternoon and then declined to say anything about itself. **That last one had no test until the reintroduction pass found it** -- the fix was already in and putting the bug back failed nothing -- which is the pass earning its keep on a defect that was already fixed. Wired at `ApplyAttemptToDay`, the one function every burn in the game passes through, so no attempt anywhere can be climbed without a line; `LastWord` is the wall's door and an empty line means silence. **Still open and named**: nothing reads `lastBurn` on screen yet, there is no sound and no camera keyed off `BeatKind`, and the belayer, the partner and a comp crowd are not on this timeline. Four defects verified by reintroduction; preflight green on all thirteen.

- 2026-08-24 - **Phase 7 pass 2: habits, and the quirks they harden into** (`Sim/DirtbagHabits.*`, SAVE v34). The back half of the character system, which Phase 7 named and deliberately did not build: *"quirks, habits and personality drift are all earned from a tally of how you actually climb, which needs instrumentation in every session and is a coherent second pass."* One rule carries the whole file: **a habit is what you have been doing lately and it can change; a quirk is what you turned out to be and it does not.** A habit is *derived* from a decaying logbook and never stored -- stop doing the thing and it goes, which is what makes it a habit rather than a badge -- and a quirk is stored, permanent, and earned exactly one way: by holding the habit for two seasons until it stopped being something you were doing. **The trap is the point**: the Grinder who hardens into Obsessive keeps the beta bonus for the rest of their career *and keeps the head penalty too*, long after they have stopped grinding. Nine habits, each a ratio in the logbook and each with a cost as well as a gift -- never warming up gets you strong and gets you hurt, plastic gets you fingers and a head that has never been anywhere, the grinder and the magpie are each other's cost because one learns lines and the other learns crags -- plus six **picked** quirks at creation, in lanes no earned quirk touches. `Pick` refuses an earned one, because **choosing to be obsessive at the counter is not the same thing as becoming it over two seasons** and that difference is the only rule here. **Nothing touches send odds**: Phase 7's rule is that only a flaw may, and an earned trait has even less business there, because a habit that made the moves easier would be a game telling you how to play. **The gate is that different careers become different people, and it passes**: three seeds under three policies, the gym member ends up *impatient, obsessive, a plastic merchant and a morning person* with a habit on every single one of 10,950 days, the sporadic outdoor climber holds one about one day in eight and ends with one or two, and **the trad leader is the only one who ever becomes bold.** Everybody becomes obsessive, which is honest rather than broken -- every policy this probe has grinds one line, and the logbook reads back what the probe actually does. **The bug the career measurement found is that the weather was giving people a personality.** The first version read Cautious off a session that started and produced no burns, and over thirty years that turned out to mean *the rock was never in condition*: one career's last season came out **71 days out and zero burns, every one counted as bailing**, so every career in the game ended cautious and rain is what made them that way. A day out is a day you got on something now, and the signal is **going home with skin still on your tips** -- read against the same thin-skin line the session advice uses -- because stopping while you still have something is a decision and not getting on because it rained for a fortnight is a fortnight. Before the fix every career ended cautious with a habit on 10,936 days of 10,950; after it, cautious appears once in nine careers and habit-days range from 582 to 10,942. **The system did not discriminate at all until the signal was a decision.** Two smaller finds: `atGym` has meant *a session started* since Phase 1 and both the wall and the crag set it -- nothing needed the distinction until the logbook did -- and `thinSkin` was a silent mirror for about sixty seconds before the dial checker caught it, so the day loop reads the session advice's own number rather than a copy. Fourth name collision in a fortnight, too: `Record` is already a *function* in `DirtbagComp.h`, so the logbook is called a logbook. Five defects verified by reintroduction; preflight green on all thirteen. **Still open and named**: `becameToday` is carried and no HUD says it out loud yet -- the same shape as `WorkedOn`'s returned talent, discarded at its call site since Phase 7 -- and personality *drift*, the third thing Phase 7 named, is a third pass.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 13 pass 2: a career of leading** (`Sim/tools/season.cpp` gained a `trad` policy). The gap pass 1 shipped with, named in its own note and closed the same day: **every number trad had was an attempt or a policy, never a career.** Three thirty-year careers, each run twice on the same seed -- once as the ordinary boulderer, once as a leader who saves for a rack and then climbs the buttress: sends **25/17/25 against 9/8/8**, head **61.6/66.7/62.1 against 77.6/100.0/69.9**, allround up at two seeds of three. **A trad career ticks about a third as much and ends a bolder climber**, and nothing had to be added for the second half of that -- head is trained off exposure and trad is where the exposure is, so it fell out of `ExposureAt` already being the single source the day loop reads. **Two things only a career could find.** *The top two rungs of the shelf were decoration*: measured with the probe simply buying what it could afford, all three careers bought a set of nuts on day one for $190 and never once held enough cash to consider cams -- and the reason is not the price, it is the shape of the money, because **a dirtbag's balance oscillates between about $125 and $290 for thirty years** and peak cash never approaches $640. Anything over about $300 has to be worked for on purpose, exactly as a dream does; with a policy that does, every career reaches doubles for $1,980 and **work goes from 13% of days to 14%** -- a few weeks of a thirty-year life, which is a decision rather than a wall. *And the rack count could not matter*: **not one lead in 2,622 emptied the harness**, because 8/12/18 pieces is more rack than any line in this valley needs -- a pitch takes a piece every three or four moves, so five or six -- which meant even the entry rack rationed at 1.0 and the rationing arithmetic never fired once. The whole *"you can run out of it"* half of the discipline was provable in the harness and unreachable in a played career: **the third time this project has shipped that shape**, after the craft's sacking gate and a comp probe that had never entered a comp. Sized against the pitches now (5/8/12) so a set of nuts genuinely does not cover a line and doubles genuinely do. Also fixed: the first version of the run-it-out metric asked whether the top third was unprotected, which measures soloing the *bottom* two thirds, came out zero across 2,449 leads, and **read as a system behaving itself** -- a metric that cannot fire is worse than no metric. Three things left open and written down: a full rack still never empties in a played career, head caps out at 100 in a long one, and the boulder-versus-trad comparison is not clean because the control climbs eight-move boulders and the leader climbs eighteen-move pitches -- the clean one is the buttress against the cave, and **the probe has only ever climbed Roadside.** preflight green on all thirteen.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 13: trad** (`Sim/DirtbagTrad.*`, SAVE v33). The cut ladder's last systemic rung, open since Phase 3 and deferred four times. **It is not a second climbing model** -- it is Phase 8's runout model, unchanged, reading protection that arrived by a *decision* instead of by a bolter, which is gate 3 and is where everything else comes from. `FallPenalty`, `PieceAt` and `LastPieceAtOrBelow` serve both disciplines; bolt the trad route by hand and it *is* the sport route, move for move, and a test says so. The difference is three sentences a watcher can name from the ground: the gear is a decision, it costs more than a clip (9.0 against 3.0 from a jug, 23.4 from a blank wall -- more than the move you are standing on), and you can run out of it. Measured, an eighteen-move crack for a grade-7.8 climber: sport 0.960/0.737/0.167 at V5/V6/V7 against trad 0.890/0.478/0.028 -- **about a grade**, which is the number every trad climber quotes about their own two grades. And it is two reasons rather than one, showing up on different rock: on a crack you pay in **pump** (3.7 pieces, 0.79 grades of exposure) and on a face you pay in **head** (1.4 pieces, 3.13 grades), which is why the buttress has both and why one testpiece is called *Ropeless in a Sense* and the other *Bombproof*. **Four things the measurement found, and three of them were the game telling you to do the wrong thing.** *Soloing was the strongest strategy and it was free*: `AttemptInput` arrives fully padded, below the first piece the exposure model is the crash-pad model, so a leader who placed nothing had zero exposure at every move of a pitch and paid no pump either -- **soloing sent 68.6%, leading sent 0%**, and every ordering test passed. `soloGradePenalty` is 3.2 now, the largest number in the resolver, above being fully pumped and above being injured, because **it is the only one of them that can kill you**. *The most expensive purchase in the game made you worse at climbing*: the fear model saturated at the sport number, so a piece you were certain would rip could never be more frightening than a bolt runout, a worse rack meant fewer placements meant less pump -- **nuts sent 0.651 and a double rack of cams sent 0.454.** Fixed by pricing a fall by *what is going to catch it*, on a convex curve, because **a piece you are 70% sure of is not 70% of the way from a bolt to the deck.** A bolt's trust is 1, lands exactly on `runoutGradePenalty`, and no golden vector moves. *And every placement on a crack was priced as if made off a crimp*, because a crack move's `restQuality` is zero unless the hold is a jug: **a hand-sized cam into a hand-sized crack goes in off a jam**, and until it did the bot placed 2.2 pieces in eighteen moves and leading sent zero. **The bot is a decision rather than a threshold.** It started as two quality bars and both were the wrong shape -- a quality bar cannot tell a good piece from a *useful* one, and with a 0.24 bar for the first placement the bot got a nut in at move 1 that it believed in so little the pitch came out **more frightening than soloing it**. It asks one question now, in grade units, through the resolver's own dials: is this stretch less frightening with the piece than without, by more than the pump costs. Spacing falls out of it, and so does declining rubbish rock. Two corrections worth keeping: **averaged over the stretch, not read off its far end** (a piece that leaves you runout by the top looks worth nothing however much of the stretch it saved), and **rationing raises the bar rather than stretching the lookahead** -- stretching it ran past where fear saturates, every comparison came out "as bad as it gets either way", and a leader with two pieces carried them to move ten and placed both next to each other. **Being short does not make you see further, it makes you fussier.** The gate that proves it is a decision: sensible 0.866, sew-it-up 0.000, solo 0.591 -- both extremes lose and soloing stays *tempting*, which is the shape a decision is supposed to have. **The Old Buttress** is the third crag, sixteen lines and three unclimbed, and its spread starts low and stays low because nobody bolts 5.7 in a cave: **trad is the one discipline whose entry-level lines are the famous ones.** SAVE v33 carries the rack, and so does `SessionState`, because the mirror checker's standing lesson holds -- a skipped field is not absent from Blueprint, it is erased from the sim on the next round trip, and a leader whose rack was erased solos the pitch. Nuts $190, cams $640, doubles $1,150, on **G** at the counter: the most expensive thing on any shelf and the only purchase that opens a crag rather than improving a day. Six defects verified by reintroduction; preflight green on all thirteen. **Still open, and named**: the season probe does not lead trad, so every number here is an attempt or a policy rather than a career -- the same gap that hid the World Cup's calendar and the tooth's dead end.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 12: work is a craft** (`Sim/DirtbagCraft.*`, SAVE v32). **A lever you pull for money is a lever; a trade you are getting better at is a life.** Phase 3 ported the odd-jobs board and the salaried trap and both were built and measured; what it did not port is that every job in the 2D game has a craft behind it. Ten of them -- setting, coaching, the counter, labour, trail, the camera, courier, rescue, the bar, the office -- and **three gigs were added to the board rather than three enum entries**, because a craft you cannot practise is a stat; a test walks two hundred days of the board and fails if any trade is unreachable. Flyering deliberately maps to nothing: some work is just work. **Gate one**: every trade pays back into the body or the head, and the same career with the teaching switched off and on comes out power +12 to +24, endurance +15 to +17, technique +9 to +13 -- in the lanes those trades teach and nowhere else, and fingers goes *down* because no trade teaches fingers and a stronger climber picks different routes. None of it is as good as climbing, which is load-bearing: **work that trained you as well as climbing did would make the salaried trap not a trap.** **Gate two**: something comes up on about a third of shifts -- a hold spins mid-session, a kid freezes at the top, the last drop is a hospital and you are forty minutes down -- and there are two ways to handle it, the right one harder and needing the craft you have actually built. Somebody who can do the job pulls it off fifty times in sixty; somebody who cannot manages under twenty-five, and **never zero, because a wall is not a decision.** The easy way is never wrong, never punished and never gets you anywhere: it has to be a real option or the decision is a skill check. **Gate three nearly shipped unreachable.** Standing is per trade and a bad enough run takes the gig off your board for good -- but the probe's policy only reached when the craft was there, so it botched two times in four hundred and forty and **was never sacked once in thirty years**: testable in the harness and unreachable in a played career, which is the same as not existing. Given a policy that always reaches, one to three sackings a career, and **the trade changes** -- losing setting pushes a career into labour and it is still there thirty years later, which is the gate: not a number in a menu, a different life. Two things found and fixed on the way. The work gain went straight onto the skill, making work **the one training path in the game with no headroom on it** -- thirty years of shifts is seven thousand hours, and at a flat rate that is a hundred and thirty points into a stat that stops at a hundred; through the same diminishing returns as everything else now, and clamped. And `BoardOpenToYou` was deleted for being sugar: it read well, nothing used it but its own test, and the engine wrote the same two-line loop inline -- **a second place for a rule to live is how the rule starts disagreeing with itself**, which is precisely the class the checker written an hour earlier exists to catch. Employer standing is per *trade* rather than per building, which is simpler than the 2D game and written down rather than hidden. preflight green on all thirteen; probe coverage 145 sim rules to 147.

- 2026-08-23 - **The body you walked in with** (`Sim/DirtbagBodyContext.*`, preflight checker thirteen). Found by accident half an hour after Phase 10 shipped, while answering *"so what can you do next?"* -- **`AttemptProblem`, the comp resolver, built its own `AttemptInput` and set six fields.** It never set the joints, the ailments, the comeback stage, the temperament or the rubber, and it *assigned* the odds penalty from the comp's pressure dial straight over the top of the climber's flaw. Measured, 400 boards, the same climber twice: **healthy 4.1, wrecked 4.1** -- where "wrecked" is a maxed-out finger joint, four cortisone shots, an old scar, a bad flu, an abscess and dead rubber, and where the session path gave that same body **2.2 grades** of ailment penalty and a fully degraded joint. That path serves gym comps, the circuit, the World Cup, the Games *and* league nights, which is most of the game's indoor time: **Phase 7 and Phase 10 were both invisible there**, and Phase 3's shoe economy was silently free. **Nothing could have caught it.** `check-unwired.py` proves a sim declaration is reachable and `check-doors.py` proves an engine verb is callable -- both are questions about *names*, and this is a question about **a struct being filled in.** Same shape as every written-and-never-wired this project has found, and the same shape as `Injury::staged`'s two owners the day before: two paths, one of them assembling by hand. Fixed with one struct for everything a body carries into any attempt anywhere and one function that stamps it -- **here the body, not the situation**: warmth, mats, cleanliness and the nerves of a competition are what make a comp different from a Tuesday and belong to the caller. Additive where the caller has spoken, because **a flaw and a comp's nerves are two reasons the odds are worse, not one replacing the other.** And a checker so the class cannot come back: *any function that declares a local `AttemptInput` must call `ApplyBody` on it*, with one escape that must give a reason, and struct fields skipped because a checker that cries wolf is worse than no checker. Verified by putting the bug back -- it names the file and line, and five assertions fail. What it is worth, over thirty years of a comp career: ranking at thirty years comes out **524 for a climber who looks after themselves, 267 for one who does not, and 75 for one who takes the shot and comes back early** -- a sevenfold spread that before this fix did not exist at all, because all three resolved their comps identically and the only difference was days lost. **The lesson, and it is bigger than the bug**: every checker here so far asks *does this name reach that name*, which is the wrong question for a caller that reaches the callee and hands it half of what it needed. This one was found by accident; the next of its shape will be too unless the question gets asked deliberately.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 10 pass 2: the things that are wrong with you that are not the injury** (`Sim/DirtbagAilments.*`, SAVE v31). **Phase 10 is done.** Pass 1 is one event and its consequences, and every injury in this game is something you did -- deliberately, because an injury must never be weather -- which leaves three shapes unbuilt. **Sickness is not your fault, and that is its entire job.** It is still not a die: it arrives off how you are living, so a fed, rested climber in a warm van essentially never gets ill and a hungry one sleeping cold on a wrecked body does, which is not a dice roll about them, it is a description of them. Measured over four thousand nights each: living well is most of the answer and it is not immunity, and eleven dollars of meds **nearly halves the days a career loses to being ill** (633 to 356 over thirty years) -- the cheapest good decision in the game and deliberately still a decision. **The tooth only ever goes one way**, which makes it the exact inverse of an injury, where ignoring it is sometimes fine: nothing improves it with rest, the only thing that ever has is money, and it costs more at every stage -- $70 for a filling and $780 for the root canal it becomes. It is the game's one pure test of whether you will spend on something that is not climbing. **Prehab is boring, it works, and nobody does it**: twenty minutes of a morning, a streak rather than a total, a third off the odds of a tweak and **never all of them** -- a climber who has done it every morning for a year still pops a pulley. Measured, it cut injuries at three seeds of four (7 to 1, 17 to 11, 10 to 3). **The shrink** is the only thing in the game that buys psyche back, and it does what a rest day cannot: it moves where psyche drifts back to overnight, for a month. **One measurement changed a system outright.** The tooth as first built escalated on a clock and then never resolved, so a career that would not pay sat at an abscess -- 1.2 grades and 18 energy a day, forever: **10,688 days of 10,950 with one, and four sends over thirty years against twenty-four.** Nothing in the harness could see it, because every claim about the tooth was true (it escalates, it never improves, money is the only answer) and the system was still wrong -- *how long you sit at the end of it* was unbounded. It costs less now, and **an abscess resolves itself the worst possible way: the tooth comes out**, which bounds never paying to the better part of a year of misery and a tooth rather than the rest of a career. `Teeth::lost` counts them, because a career remembers. And `PsycheFloor` was declared, tested and never called -- the shrink's whole point did nothing, and it would have shipped as a $110 button that buys what a good night's sleep does. The checker caught it, third time that class has appeared in two phases, along with two dials read by nothing: **you do not wake up at a hundred when you are ill**, and an abscess takes the morning. Four reintroductions verified; preflight green on all twelve; probe coverage 142 sim rules to 145.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 10 pass 1: the body keeps score** (`Sim/DirtbagMedical.*`, SAVE v30). Phase 3's injury was a wait with a price tag -- eleven days, physio buys six back, nothing to decide. This is the rest of it, and it turns on one sentence: **an injury hides its grade until you pay to look at it.** A climber with a sore finger does not know whether it is three weeks or three months; they know it hurts. Every decision is made without the number, and the number is exactly what would make the decision easy -- so the sim keeps the severity and hands the player a description, and buying the number is itself the first decision. Three tiers of it: guess blind, a physio's hands at $60 (close, and **not always right** -- the error is the whole fog), or the machine at $340 (exact, and the only thing that unlocks an operation, because nobody operates on a guess). Then three clocks. **The comeback is staged** -- resting, mobility, a graded return -- and it advances because you say so rather than because a timer ran out: waiting a stage out is never a gamble, pushing on early is, and the odds get worse the earlier you go. **The joint carries what you did to it**: cortisone ends the acute stage outright and marks the joint permanently, scars flare for the rest of a career and fade slowly and never to nothing, and surgery is the only thing that takes damage back off. **The policy** you either bought before you needed it or did not. All four gates pass, measured over thirty years against a control: sends came out 23/23, 29/25, **24/5**, 25/22 and 20/14 for sensible against impatient across five seeds -- never better, and at one seed the career is destroyed. The impatient climber is hurt for *fewer* days, because they come back early and take the shot, and ends with a body 2.6 times as likely to break and a fifth of the sends: **the cost is deferred, not avoided.** Insurance came out at $6,250 of premiums over thirty years against claims of $816 on a lucky body and $8,704 on an unlucky one -- right on the career that got hurt a lot and a slow expensive mistake on the one that did not. It was $26 a fortnight first, which is $20,332 against a maximum measured claim of $8,976: never right in any seed, which is not a bet, it is a tax with a story. And the undertreated path turned out to be the sharpest of the four: a career earns about $7,200 a year and ends thirty years of it with $159 in the tin, so **a policy that tries to scan every injury spends $0 in thirty years uninsured** -- it can never afford $340 at the moment it is needed -- and goes untreated on all forty-one of them. **Insurance is what puts real medicine within reach of a dirtbag**, which is the truest thing this phase says and fell out of the measurement rather than being designed in. The bug of the phase took three findings to kill and it is **two owners of one flag**: `BodyDay` counts an injury down and clears it, and the staged comeback is a second clock over the same flag. The probe found it three times in an afternoon, each louder -- **316 cortisone shots and 10,696 hurt days out of 10,950**, then **294 surgeries out of one injury** once a career had money, then a climber who clicked through the last stage coming out the other side still flagged hurt. Fixed by `Injury::staged` naming the owner, one `FinishInjury` that is the only place an injury ends, and one treatment per injury. **None of the three had a test until the probe found them, and two of the three reintroductions initially passed**: the flag was untested, and `rushedComebacks` is reset by the heal so a test reading it afterwards reads zero either way -- pinned now on the *scar*, which is where the difference is observable. Also fixed: scars faded geometrically, because the nightly fade read the weight it had just written and the floor, being a fraction of the current value, collapsed with it -- **a value that ages is a function of the date, not a field you keep editing**, the same lesson as the ranking one phase ago, in a different file, within a day. And `StagePenalty` was written, tested and never called, so the graded return was not graded, it was a longer wait with a nicer name; the checker caught it. preflight green on all twelve; probe coverage 134 sim rules to 142.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 9 pass 5: the ladder is a ladder** (`Sim/DirtbagLeague.*`, SAVE v28 and v29). Three pieces, and the first was a bug the fourth pass's probe had already measured: **the ranking was a ramp with no top.** A ten-year career peaked at **14,633 points against a top tier of 2,200**, which made two of this phase's five gates false -- a career could not fail to reach the Games, and a grade-6 climber parked at National tier won **none of 247 comps** while the ranking said they belonged there. Three causes and one idea behind all of them: **a placing was worth the same wherever and whenever you got it.** The curve was linear in how many people you beat, so fifth of nine paid fifty -- half a win, for beating nobody; points accumulated forever, so a rung cleared once was cleared for life; and a gym podium paid exactly what a National podium paid. Now: top-weighted like every real ranking table, a **rolling twelve-month record** rather than a total, and weighted by which room you were in (0.30 / 0.65 / 1.00, set so the rungs land on the 2D game's own thresholds over a year of turning up). Measured after: it peaks at 1,348 and settles at 672-1,023, **flat across ten years and thirty**, which is what a rolling window is supposed to do. `rankingPoints` is derived now, and a direct write survives exactly one morning -- the first version of the season test did that and the night tick caught it before any engine code could. The same measurement fixed the **selection committee sitting fifty times in ten years**: pass 3 put the review on the domestic season on the grounds that a season is the unit a committee works in, right about the unit and wrong about the length, because a season is seventy-three days. Once a year now, refused rather than deferred, and $900 a year rather than "$180 a season" paid five times -- the same money on an honest label. **Quals, semi, final** at Regional and above: six out of qualification, four out of the semi, a four-problem five-go final, and two things carry it -- **the score does not carry** (going through replaces the board with a fresh five half a grade up) and **the cut is read** (`Settle` skips anybody who went home, or a semi produces the qualification table again). Being eliminated is a result and not an error: out in qualification is a different day from finishing last in a final. **Leagues** are the other end of the same system: five dollars, ten goes, six named regulars, eight-week blocks, and **no ranking points at all** -- a comp is a day with a result and a league is a habit with a number, and what you chase is your own best score, which only goes up and which nobody can take off you. Both of its flaws were found by the probe in an afternoon and both were the league borrowing the comp's arithmetic without its meaning: the regulars took a flat 20-50 for turning up while the player took a hundred for a win, so the probe won **sixty-five blocks out of sixty-five**; and the personal best, priced relative to the board like a comp's, was **identical every week however good you got** -- two personal bests in five hundred and nineteen nights. The block table is fed from the scorecard now on one curve for everybody, the same fix and the same sentence as the World Cup's table two passes ago, and the league board is its own object -- a wide spread priced by the absolute grade of each problem -- so it rises with you and so does the number. Sixteen blocks of sixty-four and about one personal best a year. Also removed: **a guard that could not fire.** `out.won = place == 1 && yourPoints > 0` looked like the zero-tie rule and was not -- `LeagueTable` already sorts a climber on nothing behind everybody else on nothing -- so deleting it failed no test and changed no outcome. Second time this project has found one, and the rule is tested where it lives now. Three name collisions in one week (`WorldCupVenue`, `LeagueRegular`, and five `LeagueDials` fields shadowing `CompDials`), all the shape of `RivalDials::startAge`: a deliberate difference is indistinguishable from a drifted number until the name says so. preflight green on all twelve; probe coverage 103 sim rules at the start of the day, 134 now.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 9 pass 4: the World Cup and the Games** (`Sim/DirtbagWorldStage.*`, SAVE v27). The top of the ladder: ten real venues with real travel costs, six rounds a season, and **a field that flies whether you do or not** -- so a round you skip is not a round that did not happen, it is everybody else banking while you stayed home, and the season is decided by which rounds you could afford. Then the Games on a four-year cycle, gated at Olympic Hopeful, against seven climbers who are all at or above the world standard. Both reuse the comp engine unchanged, because **the format is not what makes it hard** -- five problems and seven goes, exactly like a Tuesday at the gym; what differs is the roster, the board and getting there. **The design call is that the world stage is absolute and a domestic comp is not.** A gym comp is set at *your* grade plus a tier offset because a gym comp is your peers, and the board follows you up as you improve; the World Cup is set where it is set, and getting better is what closes the gap. **Reading it the other way made the entire top of the ladder unwinnable at every skill level in the game, for everyone** -- a climber at 95 skill topped 0.01 of five problems and finished thirteenth of thirteen, and so did a climber at 55 -- and **all 76,000 checks passed**, because every one of them was an ordering and orderings hold fine on a game nobody can win. Found by measuring, the same way the rival's grade-for-skill bug was found in Phase 8, and it is the same lesson twice now: **a system whose tests are all relative needs one test that is absolute.** The standard is set against what a career actually reaches rather than against a feeling -- a ten-year career at the crag lands on an allround grade of about 6.9, so the world stage sits a grade and a half above that: at 9.2 you are tenth of thirteen, at 9.9 you podium one round in five, at 10.6 you win two in five. The Games needed correcting the other way: a softer board put a grade-9.9 climber on the medals **44%** of the time against a 19% World Cup podium, because a podium is three of eight there and three of thirteen here -- level boards put both at about 19%, so **a medal is as hard as a World Cup podium and getting to the start line is what makes it rarer.** **The probe had never entered a comp.** `Sim/tools/season.cpp` gained a `comper` policy, and until it existed the whole comp system -- the gym comp, the circuit, the tiers, the team, the World Cup, the Games -- was measured only by the harness; the measured game was not the played game at the scale of an entire subsystem, and no checker could see it because every door existed and nothing that plays a career ever opened one. It immediately found **two calendars nothing in the harness could have caught**, because both are about *how often* and a harness assertion asks *whether*: the Games came round every 56 days (**sixty Games in a ten-year career**; now 1460, the cycle everybody knows, so a thirty-year career sees seven) and a World Cup season lasted eleven weeks (**forty-seven seasons and 281 rounds in ten years**; now about a hundred days of competing and a 240-day off-season). It also found three things **reported rather than fixed**, because they are pass 2's and pass 3's dials: a ten-year career peaks at **14,633 ranking points against a top tier of 2,200**, the selection committee **sits fifty times**, and a grade-6 climber parked at National tier **wins none of 247 comps** -- all one root, ranking accumulating on a domestic clock that runs five seasons a year. **The doors are the gym wall**, all three ladders on one key in the order the day matters -- the Games, then a World Cup round, then the Tuesday comp -- and `CanFly` and `CanEnterTheGames` answer the whole question sim-side, because a rule that lives in a UFUNCTION cannot be tested from the harness and every rule this project has left in one has been found late.

- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 9 pass 3: the national team is a roster, not a threshold** (`Sim/DirtbagTeam.*`, SAVE v26). `NATL_TIERS` has read *"National Team"* at 700 points since the ranking existed and it was **a word on a progress bar**. Now it is what the 2D game's own note describes: *"a roster with your name typed on it, five other people who are also on it, a head coach who has opinions about you, a stipend that doesn't cover rent, and a review at the end of every domestic season that can quietly take all of it back."* **Two lines rather than one**: clear 700 to be named, and once on you hold all the way down to 560 -- the grace a committee actually gives a returning athlete, and without it a season spent hovering around the number is a coin flip taken five times. **The test for that started as a tautology** (`holdAt < selectAt` catches the dials being collapsed and nothing else) and now checks the thing that matters: **the same number treats an insider and an outsider differently**, so at exactly 560 somebody already on the paper stays and somebody outside is not let in. **Your teammates are people you already know** -- the top of the national field, the exact climbers you have spent a career chasing up the standings rather than five names invented for the occasion, each carrying a role read off what they are known for and which way they are going: the crimper, the engine, **the junior**, **the veteran**. The roster rebuilds at every review, so people come and go under you whether or not your own status changed, and the game keeps the names of the ones who are not on it any more -- including **the climber you went past to get on it.** **Getting the call once never un-happens**: being cut takes the spot, not the fact, coming back is worth less than arriving was, and the review says so without editorial, because a committee does not explain itself and neither does this. **One units bug caught before it shipped, and it is the same one twice now.** The 2D game's rep values here are **16, 6 and 6** on its own scale running to hundreds; the port's standing runs **-1..1**, where `DirtbagFactions.h` says outright that *"0.1 is a small deliberate act, 0.3 is a big one"*. Carrying them across unconverted would have shifted the Scene **by sixteen on a scale where one is the whole range** -- the rival's grade-for-skill bug wearing a different hat, and available **every time a number crosses between two systems that both call their axis "reputation"**. 0.40 / 0.15 / 0.15, with 0.40 deliberately above what the factions file calls a big deliberate act, and the conversion written into the dial comment rather than done silently. **The committee sits at a circuit season's close and nowhere else** -- a domestic season is the unit a selection committee works in, and reviewing you every night would make the team a thermostat -- and it runs *after* the season's own podium points are banked, because those are part of the year it is looking at. **SAVE v26 carries the roster, the coach and who you went past**: without it a career would be re-announced onto the team at every season's close, forever, with a different coach each time. Six defects verified by reintroduction.
- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 9 pass 2: the circuit season and the ranking ladder** (`notes/phase9-the-comp.md`). Evan: *"Take circuit and tiers now"*. **Five comps on firm dates six to eight days apart, and the last one is the finals** -- not a sixth event but the same format worth half as much again, which is the only thing that makes peaking for a date a decision. Firm is the point: a schedule you can plan around is the whole difference between a comp and a random event, and it is what makes *not* turning up a decision rather than an accident. **Everybody scores, not just you** -- a table that only tracked your points would be a personal best with other names printed near it, and the rival can take a season off you while you are away at a crag. **The placement curve replaced a shortcut that would not have worked**: pass 1 fed the ranking with the comp's prize `rep`, 10 for a win, against tiers gating at 350 and 1200 -- thirty-five wins to reach Regional. The real curve is **1st takes 100 and the back of the field still takes 5**, linear in *how many people you beat* rather than where you finished, so a big field is worth more to win and stepping up a tier is attractive rather than merely harder. The rep did not go away, it went where it belonged: **`rep` is what the scene thinks and feeds standing; ranking points are what the federation records**, two different numbers about the same afternoon. **Six named tiers with the 2D game's numbers pinned by a test rather than left to drift**, because 700 is where a national team calls you and 1200 is where the Games become reachable -- moving them later would move two unbuilt systems silently. Not turning up hands the rival **sixty** and costs you standing; the asymmetry is the commitment. **Two real bugs found while wiring it.** The night-tick forfeit fired the day after **every** comp, **including the ones you entered and won**, because it checked only whether the season was unfinished and one done is still fewer than five -- *what separates a comp you climbed from one you skipped is not the date, both are in the past, it is whether the ledger caught up with the calendar*, which `CompsDueBy` now counts. And `CloseSeason` guarded each podium branch on *"you scored something"*, which reads as prudence and was dead: the season table already sorts a zero behind every other zero, so a career that entered nothing is last and never reaches those branches. **Reintroducing the missing guard changed nothing and failed no test** -- the honest signal that it was never the mechanism -- so it went, because two mechanisms for one invariant is how they drift. **Doors**: the standings and your rank in the HUD's slow lines, both **silent until you have entered something**, because *"Unranked, 120 to the next"* on day one is a progress bar for a system the player has not met; and a season closes the moment the finals are turned in rather than at Sleep, since hearing about it tomorrow morning would be the game telling you something you watched happen. **SAVE v25 carries the season, dates and all** -- without it you would wake up in a season with no schedule and the night tick would forfeit its way through the year. Six defects verified by reintroduction; one did not compile the first time, the same `-Werror` trap as Phase 8, caught each time now.
- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 8's two open gates, closed — and the balance finding is bigger than either** (the race, `AcceptTheRival`/`DeclineTheRival`, SAVE v23). Evan: *"Do them next"*. **Gate 1 needed a race.** A rival who only takes lines removes them from the world, and measured that changed nothing: **1.38 first ascents with or without one**, because nothing about them made you *choose* differently. A race does — they are on a named line, you have until a stated day, and everything else you might climb this week becomes a decision. Five days, from the 2D game and kept, because **a fortnight is a background hum you would have got round to anyway and two days is a coin toss the weather decides**. Forty percent are for an **open project**, and those cannot be undone: they finish it, their name goes on it by the same path as any other ascent of theirs, and the head-to-head takes two and a half times the hit. **To measure the gate I had to build a player who answers**, because every policy in the probe is a fixed heuristic and none of them reads the rival — which is precisely *why* the rival measured as changing nothing. `racer` is `projector` with one branch: drop what you are on and go to the contested line while the clock runs. **Thirty years, 24 seeds: +34% first ascents (1.29 against 0.96), 45× the races won (5.46 against 0.12), and 76% more first ascents net.** Gate 1 passes — the rival changes what you climb and answering is worth doing. **Gate 3 was a door problem and mine.** `WouldPartnerUp` was wired and the engine set `allied = true` in the same breath, so **you could not decline** — the no-door bug with the door installed and nailed open. **C takes it, F does not**, at the van and *only* at the van, because C/F belong to the card table at the fire and a key that means *call the lie* in one trigger and *take a partner for life* in the next is how somebody agrees to rope up trying to fold a hand. **Declining costs nothing** — no standing, no head-to-head, no line of reproach, because the game does not have an opinion about which of you was right — and what it changes is that they go back to racing you, which is the whole point: saying no keeps the rival. Walking away leaves the offer standing, since *"I have not decided"* is a real answer to this one. **And the finding that outranks both gates**: **502 races in thirty years, and even the player who drops everything wins 5.5 of them** — a one percent win rate. `raceChancePerDay = 0.10` puts one every fifteen days, so a third of all days have a race running, which is not an event but weather; and `raceDays = 5` against a crag shut for rain, skin that runs out and a line at your limit means most are lost before they start. **The 2D game uses the same two numbers**, so this is not a porting error — it is what they do in a build whose weather closes the crag far more. Recorded rather than tuned, because it is a taste call about how often you should be losing and the gate measurement is a paired comparison that would not move either way. **Two more parity fixes, both the shape Phase 8 already found once**: the probe now runs the race (without it every number above would have been from a game with no races in it), and the probe now calls `YouWonTheRace` — before that it climbed the contested line and still recorded the race as lost, reporting the racer as winning 1.4, the same as a player who never noticed.
- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 8: §4 ports the route and calls it the person** (`Sim/DirtbagRival.*`, `notes/phase8-somebody-to-beat.md`, SAVE v22). *"projecting/nemesis tracking"* has been built for weeks -- `nemesis` is **the unsent line you have fed the most burns** -- and the 2D game's **rival** is a different thing entirely: a named climber who was already here when you arrived, whose grade tracks a step ahead of yours, who **takes first ascents and keeps their name on them**, who ages on their own clock and retires *into* the scene rather than out of it, and who is replaced because somebody always steps up. The port had partners, factions, a crew, a scene and a guidebook, and **nobody to lose to**. **Two things it deliberately does not duplicate.** Taking a line runs through the Lot's own machinery -- `TheyPutUpTheLine` is keyed on a *name* rather than a `Partner`, written that way for the neighbours and exactly what a rival needs, so `AsAClimber` hands back a Partner built from their grade and their ascent reaches the guidebook by the path a neighbour's takes. Two rolls for *somebody got there first* would drift, and this project has already shipped that bug once. And ageing is `DirtbagAge`'s shape rather than a second curve. **What makes them personal rather than generated**: their style leans toward **whatever you are weakest at**, so weak fingers draws a crimp assassin and no engine draws an endurance machine; **they cannot take the top of the ladder** (`gradeCap = 17`, because a rival who could reach V18 would eventually take the one thing a career is for); and **they chase rather than teleport**, so going past them buys a season of being ahead instead of one frame. Rivalry moves on first ascents, since comps are Phase 9 and an FA is the highest stake this build has. **Measured over sixteen thirty-year careers**: 2.44 rival generations, **every career watches one retire**, and 0.44 lines lost to them. **Two gates pass and two do not, and both failures are recorded rather than argued around.** Gate 4 passes outright. Gate 2 passes structurally and is **thin** -- over half of all careers never lose a line at all, which is now a tuning call with data behind it. **Gate 1 does not pass and the probe cannot pass it**: your first ascents measure **1.38 with or without a rival**, because they remove lines from the world and nothing makes you *behave* differently -- every probe policy is a fixed heuristic and none reads the rival. It needs a **race** (the 2D game has one) or a human. **Gate 3 does not pass and it is my fault**: `WouldPartnerUp` is wired but the engine sets `allied = true` in the same breath, so **you cannot decline** -- the no-door bug with the door installed and nailed open. **Eight defects verified by reintroduction**, and **four findings about the tests rather than the code.** A reintroduction that tripped `-Werror=unused-function` **never ran the suite and printed no failures**, which reads exactly like a test that does not catch it -- CLAUDE.md warns about this and it still took two attempts. A save test asserted a size and then indexed regardless, so dropping the count **segfaulted the harness** and took the rest of the suite with it. `RivalDials::startAge` shared a name with `AgeDials::startAge` -- 26 against 24 -- and the dial checker read a deliberate two-year gap as one drifted number; it was right to, because a reader cannot tell a deliberate difference from an accidental one when the fields are called the same thing, so it is `rivalStartAge` now with the gap written down as *why* they are a benchmark: **their lead is a head start rather than talent.** **And the one that matters**: `AsAClimber` passed the rival's **grade** (0-18) to a parameter expecting a **skill** (0-100), so the rival was a beginner who took **zero first ascents in thirty years** while ageing, peaking, retiring and being succeeded entirely convincingly -- and **nothing in the suite noticed**, because every test asked whether the machinery ran rather than whether it did anything. Found by measuring rather than testing. `GradeToSkill` now lives beside `SkillToGrade`, because two copies of one conversion is how that happens. **The probe runs the rival too**, for the reason checker eleven exists: the engine takes lines for them, so a probe that did not would make every Phase 8 measurement a measurement of a game nobody plays.
- 2026-08-23 - **Boldness gets wired, and the measurement outranks the wiring** (`NerveShift`, `AttemptInput::boldness`, `notes/phase7-who-you-are.md`). Debt from this morning, closed the same day. Boldness shifts the resolver's `nerve` -- **head is how well you handle being up there, boldness is how much it bothers you**, different questions that add -- and it is not a buff, because the *temperament* is the choice and the axis is not: Send-or-Bust buys **+55 boldness with -30 discipline**, so it commits to more and keeps less of every session it ever has. Both halves are pinned. **But measuring it turned up something about the fear model that matters more.** Exposure is the only thing nerve ever spends, and it comes out at **0.900 grade units on unpadded rock, 0.367 on a rope, and exactly 0.000 once you own pads** -- with nerve worth half of that at most. So boldness is felt **at your limit, on unpadded ground, and essentially nowhere else**: +0.2% two grades below, +1.8% one below, **+10.4% at the limit**, and zero above it because you fall off before you ever reach the height. That is a good sentence about climbing rather than a fault -- *fear costs you exactly when the margin is thin* -- and the last part is not new, it is `mostFoamCanDo` measured back in Phase 3 where *"a well-padded highball is still a highball"* was the deliberate call. It does mean **a bold temperament is worth nothing at all to a boulderer who owns pads**, which somebody should know before tuning it. **A real bug fell out on the way**: `ShiftPayMultiplier` clamped purism at zero, so **a purist worked for less and a pragmatist worked for exactly the same** -- the whole negative half of the axis was free, and the Influencer at -40 purism was collecting the boldness, the audience and full pay for a -10 discipline ticket. And the check that caught it then failed for a second reason worth keeping: **a Purist who Sold It All still clears 1.0, because a 12% CV beats an 8% conscience**, so the absolute test needed an origin whose own pay lane is neutral or the perk sits on top of the axis and hides its sign. **The first boldness test was worthless and the reintroduction is what said so**: deleting the single line that plumbs `NerveShift` into `AttemptInput` **passed the entire suite**, because the test only checked that `NerveShift` computed different numbers and never that anything read them -- this project's oldest bug wearing a test's clothes. Two more test failures on the way to a real one, and both were the test rather than the code: the first measured on a 5.12a pitch where the mean highpoint was **0.27 of sixteen moves**, so the climber fell off the first move every time and never reached the exposed ground; the second hard-coded grade 5, which is inside the limit band for one generated route and outside it for another. It walks the grades now and measures at the first one where a cautious climber sends between a tenth and three quarters of the time, which is what *at your limit* means. **`social` stays unwired and the reason is recorded rather than deferred**: it is *whether people turn up*, and **nobody ever fails to turn up** -- `LotRegulars` hands back all three regulars, every day, for ninety years. There is nothing for the axis to bend until turnout exists, and turnout is the roadmap's own standing design question rather than a wiring job.
- 2026-08-23 - **Phase 7: every climber in this game was identical at birth** (`Sim/DirtbagCharacter.*`, `notes/phase7-who-you-are.md`, SAVE v21). `NewClimber` rolled five skills within +/-6 of fifty and a body type, and that was the whole of a person. Now: **where you came from, what kind of climber you are, what is wrong with you, what you are like** -- and **one gift and one anti-talent rolled behind your back**, live from the first move and discovered only by working that lane for a season. **Two rules, and only one is zero-sum.** Archetype offsets sum to zero for every archetype, so choosing one is choosing a shape rather than a score -- a rule that **arrives already paid for**, since the 2D game learned it on its competition field, measured the version without it and fixed it by shifting the offsets. **Origins deliberately do not sum to zero**, because where you came from is a life and not a build: each holds one permanent perk in a lane **no other origin touches** (shift pay, indoor gains, training gains, daily costs, shop prices, physio), and **none goes near send odds** -- only a flaw is allowed there, and a test asks every origin for its odds penalty and requires zero. **The bug this phase found in itself is the finding.** The first version had no `built` flag, and `Build` has to default to *something*: it defaulted to an All-Rounder who Sold It All with Gumby, which meant -- measured, not guessed -- **every existing caller trained technique at half rate and got paid 12% more per shift**, the probe and the golden vectors and every measurement in every note in this repo included. **All 75,027 checks passed**, because the training tests are *orderings* and halving both sides preserves an ordering. That is this project's own `magnitude vs ordering` lesson arriving from a direction nobody was watching: not a test too weak for a new feature, but a test exactly strong enough until a default changed underneath it. An unbuilt character is nobody now, nobody gets no modifiers, and every effect is pinned at **exactly** neutral -- which also hands old saves a free answer, since a v20 career migrates to *unbuilt* and loads with the numbers it was measured with rather than being ambushed by a creation screen twenty years in. **Measured over 24 paired seeds**: a Boulderer out-grades a Rope Gun **24/0/0** at 5.99 against 4.85 while their average of five skills is **identical to two decimal places** -- exactly what a zero-sum table should produce, and the strongest evidence the rule works. **Sends turned out to be the wrong metric and the note says why**: unpaired across twelve seeds one build scores anywhere from **0 to 25**, so whether a season produces sends is mostly whether the seed's crag has a line at your grade. Flaws bite where they say -- Tweaky Fingers is **+48% injuries over a year** -- but **diminishing returns eat most of a rate flaw**: Gumby trains technique at *half rate* and lands only 5% lower after a year, so the risk flaws are much stronger than the rate ones while the blurbs promise them as equals. Recorded rather than tuned. Origins spread ten points of power on day one, about a grade and a half, and **end-of-year cash does not separate at all** (11/2/11, $159 against $162) -- which is not a Phase 7 bug but the treadmill this project already measured, where 98% of everything earned goes straight back out. **The perk lands somewhere better than a cash column**: the Trust-Fund Kid climbs on **19% less worn rubber**, because 30% off means replacing it sooner, and dead rubber is the thing that costs sends. Not designed -- it fell out of the economy. **The door is four questions on the handover's furniture**, because in this game a new career *is* an arrival and the first climber and the one who turns up after you retire are answering the same question. Keys 1-6, three of them new, because six origins need six keys and a question offering an option with no key to press is the bug the wall's ethics prompt had two days ago. **Two of the four personality axes have no reader**: `social` and `boldness` want seams in the partner and sport models this phase does not touch, so they are stored, saved and checked but not consumed -- recorded rather than shipped as two multipliers nothing multiplies. Quirks, habits and drift are all earned from a tally of how you actually climb and are a coherent second pass. **Verified by reintroduction, ten defects**, including one that mattered more than it looks: the save dropping the character, without which `bBuilt` loads false and **the creation screen reopens on every load, forever.**
