After the ninth it stopped being worth finding them one at a time, so the question is asked for every public sim declaration at once: **is this reachable from the engine?** It found **thirteen more.** The rule is "reachable from `Source/`", transitively, and **the season probe is deliberately not a root** — that distinction is the whole tool: `FactionDay` was called by the probe every simulated day and by the engine never, so the mechanic measured as working in detail for months while in the actual game a crag pulled access once and never gave it back. What is unreachable: **driving is free** (`FuelFor` was reachable from the probe and not from the engine, and fuel was billed as "the only cost that goes up the more you climb") *[corrected 2026-08-21: first written as "no caller anywhere", which was wrong — `season.cpp` has always charged it, which is where the $918–$1,224 came from. Engine-only, like the rest of the list.]*; **the salaried-job trap does not exist** (`WorkSalariedDay` — the engine can ask `SalariedToday()` and has no way to work the day); **a sponsored player is never paid and never reviewed** (`MonthlyStipend`, `ReviewSeason` — the $640/month tier pays $0); **"no partner, no pitch" is unenforceable** (`NeedsABelayer` is wired, so the game can ask the question and cannot answer it); a partner lifts no psyche and standing buys no beta. And one that is worse than unwired: `ShoePenalty` has no caller **because the resolver carries its own inline copy of the same formula** — two copies of one question, the exact drift this project has written three separate rules against. **No measurement changes**: every probe result in these notes runs against the sim directly, where all of this does execute, so those numbers stand. What changes is what "built" means — Phase 3 and Phase 4 are recorded with their named scope complete, and the sim's half genuinely is. Each gap now carries an `unwired-ok: NOT WIRED — …` line at its declaration and preflight prints the count on every run, so "0 problems" can never come to mean "nothing to do". Two of the nine were fixed on the way: **`CreditFirstAscent` had no caller**, so every first ascent in the game was worth no opinion to any faction — `ClaimFirstAscent` is now the whole verb and the engine calls it; and **`CoversShoes` had no caller**, so the bottom sponsorship rung, whose entire content is free shoes, gave the player nothing — `Resole`/`BuyNewShoes` now take who is paying as a **parameter with no default**, because that is the only version that cannot go quiet again.
- 2026-08-21 - **Head: the skill read everywhere and written nowhere** (`ExposureAt`, `headExposureRate`, `notes/phase4-head.md`, **no save version needed**). The skin sweep found `head` gaining **+0.00 in every run at every setting** — exactly zero, always. It was *read* in four places (the resolver twice, the sport runout, and the age model that names it one of two skills that never decline) and trained in none, so a player's head was frozen at birth and "never declines" was true in the emptiest possible way. The design was not invented here: `concepts/DIRTBAG.md` already said **"Psyche / head — fall-commitment beats above gear."** And the sim already priced exactly that, inside the resolver, as a local it computed and threw away every move. It is now `ExposureAt(route, index, padding)` — *how bold is this move, in grade units* — called by the resolver to price a scary move and by the day loop to train head off it, **one source, because two copies of "how bold was that" drift the way two copies of `SkillToGrade` would.** The golden vectors are untouched by the extraction, which is what says it was faithful. Head gains on **the boldest move you actually committed to**, and three things are deliberate: peak rather than sum (twenty moves along a padded traverse teach nothing), **falling counts** (it reads the highpoint reached, not whether it went — a fall from above the bolt teaches the lesson at least as well), and it sits **outside the challenge/engagement gate the other four are behind**, because head is the one skill that does not train on trying hard. It cannot be farmed: exposure is zero on move one, zero under full pads, zero below the first bolt. **And it gives kit a price for the first time.** A year, 8 seeds, varying only pads: **0 pads → +3.76 head, 1.6 sends; one pad → +1.78, 3.0; two pads → +0.00 head, 5.5 sends.** The second pad was previously a pure +37% upgrade with no downside — the one purchase measured to matter, and it mattered for free. Now **pads buy sends and cost head**, which is the trade a real boulderer makes at the shop. Two pads meaning zero exposure forever would freeze a career, except that pads are not the question on a rope: above the first bolt the runout takes over, so **the cave is where a safe boulderer gets their head back** — emergent rather than arranged, and pinned by a test. Rate sized against the table, not by feel.
- 2026-08-21 - **What binds, if not skin — and option A is dead** (`notes/phase3-what-binds.md`). Five measurements had ended at the same wall and left two options: **stop skin being the sole cap**, or **restate the gate**. Both were guesses. Swept `skinRegenPerNight` 1.5 → 9.0 across 12 seeds; nothing shipped changed. **Lifting skin does not add climbing, it adds injuries** — days at the crag go *down* 181 → 121 while injuries go 0.0 → 4.2 and days hurt 0 → 90, and `days + hurt` stays flat at 181–211 the whole way. A six-fold skin budget buys **54% more burns**. So skin was never the wall: **it is the guardrail in front of it.** Shot skin is what stops you before your tendons do, and the load system — dormant since it was built, because Phase 4 measured 0 injuries in twelve outdoor seasons — becomes the binding constraint the moment skin stops binding. And the gate **does not close at any skin setting**: the `kept` control (no bills, no food, no fuel, never works a day) out-sends the working player in **8 of 72 seed-pairs**, including at the setting where skin cannot bind at all. Deleting the entire cost of being alive buys no climbing however much skin you have, so **option A is measured and refuted, and restating the gate is the remaining honest move.** Two defects fell out. **`head` gains exactly 0.00 in every run at every setting** — `TrainingWeights` carries power, fingers and technique and head is not in the training model at all, while being *read* by the resolver twice, by the sport runout code, and by the age system that calls it one of two skills that never decline. Seventh thing written and never wired, and the first that is a whole skill axis: read everywhere, written nowhere. Left unfixed on purpose — what trains head is a design question, not a dial. And **the probe has been reading the wrong skill**: `grade` is `SkillToGrade(power)`, the *slowest* grower (+2.4 a year against fingers' +7.6), so every season number in these notes reads a third of a grade where the truth is about half. New `allround` column; `grade` left alone so old notes stay comparable to their own numbers.
- 2026-08-21 - **The seven HUD lines, and a sixth thing written and never wired.** The desk list asked Evan to *"drop seven Blueprint nodes on the HUD"*. There is no HUD Blueprint — `ADirtbagHUD::DrawHUD` is C++ drawing to the canvas — so that was twenty minutes of editor work that could not have been done, standing in front of the load warning, which is **worth 37% of a season's sends**. All seven are wired in code now. The rule they are wired by: **a line that is always on screen is a line you stop reading**, so each one stays silent until it is saying something. Load says nothing below 40 and `IsHurt` gates the injury line, `StandingText` was already silent below ±0.3, and sponsor's "nobody is calling" is true and not worth a row. Age, kit and sponsor sit bottom-left with the career line, because none of them change in a day. `LoadWarning` was added to the sim rather than thresholds to the HUD: **the colour and the sentence are two readings of one fact**, and a presentation layer holding its own copy of `injuryThreshold` is how a warning ends up going amber two seasons after the dial moved — the test moves the dial and checks both bands move together. And **`EthicsNews` was drawn nowhere**: `Sleep` has been setting it since the ethics commit, so the day your chipped hold came out, the game knew and said nothing. Sixth of this shape after `KitDay`, the body roll, `FactionDay`, the legacy save path and ethics discovery — the first one where the thing was wired and simply never *drawn*.
- 2026-08-21 - **The book never got written into** (`WriteIntoTheBook`, `notes/phase2-book-writeback.md`). Found in a real save: Evan named his first ascent `Bouncin` and reported *"i didnt notice the name in game."* The ledger had done its half - `given=Bouncin`, `fa=1`, `confirmed=3` - and **nothing ever wrote it onto the page.** `NameFirstAscent` takes the crag line `const` by design, because `route.name` is the ledger key and moving it would orphan every attempt recorded against it; but no other code wrote the line either, so `CragLine::displayName` was documented as "what the book shows" and set by nobody, ever. The guidebook went on calling it *project, the slab right of the pull-off, the book guesses V3 and nobody has confirmed it* - about a line with a name, a first ascent and a confirmed grade three fields away. **And none of the crag is saved**: it is regenerated from the world seed on every venue change, so the ascent lives only in `ProjectMemory` and a walk to the cave and back would have wiped it regardless. `WriteIntoTheBook` rehydrates the page from the ledger - given name, who did it, `isProject = false`, and `route.grade = confirmedGrade`, the book's guess becoming a fact, which is the whole payoff of the mechanic - and runs both in `EnsureCrag` (so it survives a reload) and at the moment of naming (so it is true while the player is still standing in front of it). Two more halves of the same silence: the wall's `RouteName` was a `BeginPlay` snapshot, so a line named mid-session kept its old name for the life of the actor, and **nothing ever said the name back to you** - the HUD had always drawn "FIRST ASCENT — yours to name" and had no other half, so you typed a name, the widget closed, and the world carried on. The tests missed it because `TestNamingIsEarnedAndExact` checks the ledger exhaustively and never looks at the book: the bug lived entirely in the gap between two things that were each correct. The new test asserts the negative first.
- 2026-08-20 - **Ethics: the things you did, and the day somebody finds out** (`Sim/DirtbagEthics.*`, `notes/phase4-ethics.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 14**). The brief was *multi-beat arcs whose consequences resurface seasons later*, and that clause is the design: factions already price what you do openly, on the day. This is the other kind of act. An ethical shortcut is a **secret with a fuse** — you take the benefit now and carry a thing that can come out, and **the more people are watching you, the more likely it is that somebody noticed.** Measured, for chipping one hold: a nobody has a **50% chance it never comes out at all** across thirty years; a star is found out at **5.2 years median, 2% never**. That gap is the whole mechanic — success is what exposes you, and the person caught is not quite the person who did it. Five acts, and the split that matters: chipping and retro-bolting change the rock so the ascent stands (hollow, on a line that is not what it was), while claiming, staging and pulling on gear are lies about what happened and **the send is stripped from the record** — which cascades into a sponsor next review. One thing surfaces at a time, because a career unravelling in an afternoon is a punishment rather than a story. Discovery is called from `Sleep` and lands in `EthicsNews` beside `DogWorry`: you wake up and everyone already knows.
- 2026-08-20 - **A test that passed for the wrong reason.** `TestAFreshSecretIsQuiet` ran four hundred iterations against one world seed, but discovery derives from (world, day, index) — so all four hundred rolled the *same forty-five numbers*. It looked like four hundred samples, was forty-five, and passed with the guard it tested deleted. Caught by injecting that exact defect; the seed varies per iteration now and the reason is written into the test.
- 2026-08-20 - **Sponsorship, and the fifth time the same wall** (`Sim/DirtbagSponsor.*`, `notes/phase4-sponsor.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 13**). Every other source of money competes with climbing; this one arrives *because* of it, which made it the last untried answer to the Phase 3 gate. The mechanic: **a shift lands on any day, a photo shoot lands on a good one** - `ObligationToday` returns false on any day without a window, always, because you cannot shoot climbing photos in the rain. So a sponsor takes precisely the days you wanted. What earns a deal is what people can **see** - sends, first ascents, and the Scene - not ability, which makes the Scene axis load-bearing for the first time since it was built. Three rungs: free rubber for nothing, $120/mo for one good day a month, $640/mo for three. Being hurt pauses the review clock rather than running it, and a failed review costs a rung rather than the career. **Measured over five-year careers: a title deal pays you out of the workforce entirely - 21% of days worked down to 0%, $39,040 - and takes about 27 good days a year, and the send count does not move at any tier on any seed.** Fifth independent confirmation of the same wall, and the cleanest statement of it yet: **the binding constraint was never money and was never time. It is skin.** A season has 208 days with a window and a player can climb about 130 before their skin is gone, so losing 27 of the other 78 costs nothing and $39,000 buys nothing. Phase 3's gate cannot be closed by any money mechanic - bills, fuel, the kit, physio and sponsorship have all been tried. The two honest moves are to **stop skin being the sole cap**, or to **restate the gate**, because "sends per year" may be the wrong metric when a year's texture changes completely and its outcome does not. **Evan's call, not mine.**
- 2026-08-20 - **`patch.py` gained `edits()`, earned the hard way.** `edit()` raises on a bad anchor, which kills the script - so every edit written after the failing one silently never runs. Three broken states in this project came from exactly that: a printf that gained values but not format specifiers, a migration registered nowhere, and a save that wrote four fields it never read back. All three compiled cleanly. `edits()` checks every anchor first and writes nothing unless all of them match, so a partial application is not a state the repo can reach.
- 2026-08-19 — **Retiring, and what a career leaves behind** (`Sim/DirtbagLegacy.*`, `notes/phase4-legacy.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 12**). Aging let a career decline; it could not *end*, and a climber who cannot stop is a treadmill rather than a career. The design call: **the world remembers, the body does not.** Nothing physical carries — the next one is twenty-four with nothing in their fingers, one pad and $300, because inheriting somebody's tendons would make the second life a save-scum of the first. What carries is everything outside the body: **your lines are in the guidebook under the names you gave them**, the Lot knows whose van that is (35% of standing, *signed* — inherit a career that annoyed the stewards and you arrive already annoying them), and the gate opens, because a crag shut forever is a dead crag rather than a consequence. **Nobody is ever thrown out**: retirement is offered on two honest triggers — three injuries back to back at any age, or two grades off your best past 46 — and **age alone is never the reason**, so a 49-year-old still climbing at their peak is told nothing. `ProjectMemory` learned which ladder its line is on, because a card that cannot tell 5.13c from V7 confidently prints the wrong one. Tested against a real v11 fixture generated by the v11 writer rather than hand-typed.
- 2026-08-19 — **A fourth thing written and never wired.** The engine's save path took `(Seed, Player)` and nothing else, so every legacy filed on the game instance would have been written to disk **exactly never** — and nothing would have looked wrong until somebody opened the guidebook and found three generations missing. Same shape as `KitDay`, the body roll and `FactionDay`. Fixed the same way: `SaveGameToFile`/`LoadGameFromFile` are now the complete path with the game instance routed through them, and the narrower Blueprint pair is documented as first-life-only.
- 2026-08-19 — **The Shaded Cave, and the belayer** (`ShadedCave`, `Sim/DirtbagSport.*`, `notes/phase4-cave.md`). After building the sport system there was **not one sport route in the game** — all thirty guidebook lines are boulders, so the rope code was unreachable, exactly where the town was that morning. So: **eighteen authored pitches from 5.9 to 5.14a** plus three bolted projects, forty minutes up the hill and **north-facing** — which is the point of it. Roadside is east-facing and the season model puts high summer's window at dawn and nowhere else; `SunOnRock` is zero for north aspects, so in July the cave is the only rock in the valley worth the walk. A second crag that is the answer to a season rather than more of the same. And **no partner, no pitch**: a non-climbing neighbour holds nothing, a stranger gives you 3 burns, somebody you have spent a season with gives you 12. This is the first place **rapport buys something you cannot get any other way** — beta you could get by trying more and psyche is worth well under a grade, but a redpoint burn is not available at any price without somebody who knows you. One comment corrected in place: the cave's derived stream does *not* protect Roadside's rock (BuildRoute salts by route name, so call order never mattered) — it buys name-collision safety and a stateful project stream, which is narrower and true.
- 2026-08-19 — **Sport climbing: the rope, the bolts and the runout** (`Sim/DirtbagSport.*`, `notes/phase4-sport.md`). `Discipline::Sport` had existed since Phase 0 and did almost nothing — a mid-route rest stance, and otherwise resolved exactly like a six-move boulder. Now: bolts derived from how a line is equipped, **the ground-fall penalty stops at the first bolt** (held wrong it follows a roped climber thirty metres up and taxes them for having no foam under a rope route), the **runout** takes its place as the rope head game, and **clipping costs pump** — more from a bad stance, which is why a clipping stance is a thing climbers talk about. Building it exposed a hole nobody had measured because sport was never used: a V7.8 climber sent a V7 pitch **0.9% of the time** while sending the V7 boulder 68%, because pump accrued over nineteen moves with one guaranteed rest. Two structural fixes — **a pitch has stances** (one roughly every four moves; boulders untouched), and **beta scales with length**, since flat half-a-grade quietly said learning a twenty-move pitch is worth no more than learning a six-move problem, and could not pay for fifteen compounding chances to fall. Plus one of my own caught by measuring: `clipPumpCost` at 9.0 against a base of 11.0 took sport's send rate from 65% to **zero at every grade**. After: a pitch at the climber's limit goes **15% onsight, 40% half-wired, 67% wired**, a grade below goes 73%, a grade above 8% — a curve rather than a wall.
- 2026-08-19 — **Age: what bends the curve, and what does not** (`Sim/DirtbagAge.*`, `notes/phase4-age.md`, **no save version needed**). The obvious build is to subtract numbers from an old climber, and it is the wrong one — what ends careers is not being weaker, it is **not being able to train as much**. So age acts through the load budget: recovery slows past 30 (floor 0.45) and the injury threshold falls to meet you. Three genuinely different mechanisms: **decay** (1.6 power/year past peak — outrun by turning up, catches only the player who stops), **the ceiling** (what your body will still hold however hard you train), and **the budget**. The ceiling was added *after* measuring: without it a climber who kept training **never declined at all**, pinning at 100 power from 28 to 52, because gains at the ceiling outrun decay five to one. And the asymmetry is the point — power goes first, fingers hold, endurance later, and **technique and head never decline at all**. A hard-training career measured end to end: peak **V11.4/V12.0 at 28**, still **V9.9/V10.8 at 40**, **V7.8/V8.5 at 52**, with the technical line outlasting the power line the whole way down. Age is **derived from the day counter and never stored**, so a whole system cost no migration and a save can never disagree with a birthday.
- 2026-08-19 — **The town, the jobs board and factions reach the game** (`DirtbagSimLibrary`, `DirtbagGameInstance`). Roughly 700 lines of tested sim had **no callable Blueprint surface at all** — `EDirtbagVenue` was still `{Gym, Crag}`, and there was no way to read the board, take a gig, check your standing, or ask whether a door was open. Now there is: `FDirtbagVenue`/`FDirtbagTown` mirrors with `Town`, `VenueIsOpen`, `VenuesFor`, `OpenVenueFor` and `VenueText`; `OddJobBoard` and `TakeOddJob`; `TakeSalariedJob`/`QuitSalariedJob`/`SalariedToday`; and `StandingLine`, `StandingWith` and `CragIsOpen`. Enum parity for Service, Faction and InjuryKind is now held by `static_assert` like every other pair. `VenueIsOpen` asks the sim rather than reimplementing the comparison, because two copies of "is it open" drift into a door the HUD says is open and the day loop says is not.
- 2026-08-19 — **A shut crag would have stayed shut forever.** `FactionDay` — the standing drift and the closure countdown — was called by the season probe and **never by the engine**, so in the actual game access would have been pulled once and never restored for the rest of the save. That is the **third** per-day tick written and left uncalled (`KitDay` bought 365 days of membership for one $75; the body roll was the second), so the rule is now structural rather than remembered: anything that counts down counts down inside `SleepToNextDay`, where a night is. `TestANightIsWhereEverythingCountsDown` fails on the old code and holds all three.
- 2026-08-19 — **Training load and injury: the second body budget** (`Sim/DirtbagBody.*`, `notes/phase4-body.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 11**). Phase 4 work taken out of order and deliberately: both Phase 3 measurements ended at the same wall, that skin caps the year so nothing money buys can add climbing to a season. Load is the second budget — **hard climbing accrues it, it heals over a month rather than six nights, and past 62 it is how you get hurt.** Twelve seasons per policy: an **outdoors-only climber cannot overtrain at all — 0 injuries in twelve seasons**, because 157 washed-out days and 75 rest days mean they climb one day in three. That is not a soft dial, it is a true thing about weather-limited crags. **A hangboard removes that protection**, being the one thing in the game that loads tendons without the weather getting a say. And the warning is legible before it bites: a player who stops training at "everything aches; this is the warning" sends **38 against 24**, and spends **55 days hurt against 1,467**. Injuries are specific and decide what you can still climb on — a pulley ends crimping and leaves slopers alone, a shoulder is the reverse, a lumbrical is pockets and only pockets, an elbow is everything a little and so nobody rests it properly. Nobody gets hurt out of the blue (below the threshold the chance is flat zero, never small), tendons do not tear in a camp chair (the roll only happens on a day you pulled on), and climbing on it is a gamble both ways. **Physio is money for time**, once a week at most — the second thing money buys that hands climbing back rather than moving it around, and the first one a player will actually reach.
- 2026-08-19 — **Four defects the body measurement caught.** The **hangboard accrued no load at all** — the most injury-causing activity in climbing was free, and priced in *skin*, which was never what a board costs; that stand-in existed only because no tendon budget did (skin 1.6→0.3, load 0→14). Load was **asking the wrong question**, measuring grades-over-you, so a player projecting at their limit was over by zero and a season peaked at 6 out of 100 — climbing at your limit *is* maximal intensity. **Aggravation ran away**, turning one bad fortnight into a 226-day injury; severity rises now and days are recomputed from it, so the worst an injury can be is the worst an injury is. And the probe's table had **silently empty columns twice** — a field appended to a printf's values and not its format — so every machine-readable line now carries a `HEAD` row emitted from the same place. Two signatures also became stricter on purpose: `SleepToNextDay` and `ApplyAttemptToDay` take the world RNG as a **required** parameter, because those are the two places the body rolls and a caller who forgot would silently play a game where nobody ever got hurt.
- 2026-08-19 — **The kit: what money should buy, measured** (`Sim/DirtbagKit.*`, `notes/phase3-kit.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 10**). The gate failed because there was nothing to want, so: a second **crash pad** ($260), a **hangboard** ($85), and a **gym membership** ($75/month, the only climbing that ignores the weather). Twelve seasons per policy. Buying all three is **neutral** — 43 sends against a non-owner's 38, inside a per-seed variance of 0–18 — but forcing the second pad gives **59, at the same crag days and the same burns**. That gap is the finding, and it generalises: **skin caps the year, so kit that spends skin only moves climbing around; only kit that raises the value of skin already spent can add to a season.** The pad costs no skin and makes every burn you were taking anyway more likely to land; the board and the gym both draw on the budget the crag draws on, which is exactly why `kitted` climbs 361 fewer days outside and comes out level. The board is not a trap but using it badly is: fired on any dead day it gave **0 sends across five seasons** against a non-owner's 12, and gated on genuinely surplus skin the same item at the same price gave **24 against 12** — same object, opposite sign, decided entirely by when you reach for it. And the item measured to matter is the one the probe **never once afforded** in twelve seasons: $260 against a year whose cash high is $240. That is the first time in this project that the thing worth having is the thing you cannot quite reach. Gate still not passed, but the shape of the answer is known rather than guessed.
- 2026-08-19 — **Three defects the kit measurement caught.** `KitDay` existed and was called by nobody, so a single $75 bought 365 days of membership — it ticks down in `SleepToNextDay` now, with everything else that runs out. The hangboard **out-trained the wall four to one** (1.44 finger points a day against climbing's 0.32) because nothing capped sessions and skin alone allowed eleven; now one a day at 40% of a light session. And pads defaulted to zero, which silently applied a penalty nobody could afford to remove and **took the five-seed send count from 17 to 3** — that is not a new decision for the player, it is the game quietly getting harder, so everybody starts with one pad and the second is the purchase.
- 2026-08-19 — **The Phase 3 gate, measured — and it fails, for a reason worth having** (`notes/phase3-economy.md`, **SAVE_VERSION 9**). The probe could never answer "does the money pressure the climbing" because every policy in it works whenever cash runs low; a thermostat cannot tell you how cold it is outside. So the measurement is a control: a **`kept` player with no bills, free food and free fuel**, who never works a day. Across five seeds their season is *indistinguishable* — 133 climbing days against 135, 524 burns against 522, and **fewer** sends than the player paying rent. Seventy days of work a year costs nothing. Three reasons, in order: an odd job is four hours starting at seven and the window is later, so a shift costs you the morning and the morning was never the climbing; **skin caps the year, not time** (133 climbing days out of the 208 that have a window at all, leaving 232 days to put work in); and the real one — **there is nothing to want**. The only discretionary spending in a whole season is shoes, $275 against $6,601 earned, and the year ends with $133 in the bank and nowhere for it to go. Money cannot pressure climbing because money does not *buy* climbing. Same root cause kills the faction choice: greedy and careful end the year on the same cash, so turning down the guidebook money is free and therefore not a decision. Recorded as **honestly unmet**, with the next mechanic named rather than a dial pass proposed: things worth money that make you a better climber.
- 2026-08-19 — **Seasonal daylight, and fuel** (`DaylightHours`, `FirstLightHour`, `LastLightHour`, `FuelFor`). `firstLight`/`lastLight` were pinned at 6-to-20 all year, so a nine-to-five always left three hours of evening and a salaried season climbed **as many burns as an unemployed one — 521 against 518 — for $19,900 more**. Light now swings around solar noon with the solstice leading the warmest day by thirty: **16.5 hours midsummer, 7.9 midwinter, dark at 16:26 in December** — before you clock off. And driving stopped being free: at $9 an hour (a van at eighteen to the gallon, close to the real arithmetic) a season spends $918–$1,224 getting to the crag, which is **the only cost in the game that goes up the more you climb**. Days worked moved 15% → 19%. Two probe bugs fell out: it climbed by headlamp until the skin ran out, and it drove to locked gates — a hundred pointless hours a year, free until fuel had a price.
- 2026-08-19 — **Two silent data losses, caught by a new check** (`tools/check-mirror-coverage.py`, **SAVE_VERSION 9**). The existing checkers ask whether the fields we touch exist; this one asks the opposite, and it is the one that bit. `Weather` gained a `day` and `FDirtbagWeather` did not, so `ToSim` would have handed the sim a default-constructed date and **searched every window in the game against a midwinter day, all year**, with nothing failing to compile. The same check found `PlayerState.job` and `.standing` had no engine mirror at all. Fixing the second surfaced the third: **standing was saved from v8 and the job was not**, so a career loaded its way out of employment — v9 carries it, with a migration and a hand-written v8 fixture proving the upgrade. A field deliberately not surfaced now says so with `// mirror-skip: name -- why`, next to the omission.
- 2026-08-19 — **Cleaning only costs you on virgin rock** (`Sim/DirtbagFirstAscent.cpp`). `ScrubbedALine` fired on every brush, which put a *careful* projecting player at −0.70 with the stewards having never once taken the money work — 70 days shut out of a year for maintenance. Taking a wire brush to virgin rock is development and somebody notices; going over an established line before you pull on is housekeeping and nobody has an opinion about it. Measured after: greedy −0.23…−0.95 with 0–12 closures, careful −0.05…−0.23 with none. Closures went back to being a consequence instead of weather.
- 2026-08-18 — **Factions v1, and the town as data** (`Sim/DirtbagFactions.*`, `Sim/DirtbagTown.*`, **SAVE_VERSION 8**). Four groups on **two opposed axes** — Old Guard ↔ Scene, Development ↔ Stewardship — so pleasing one costs another and there is no maxing everything. Standing attaches to things the player was doing anyway rather than to a menu: **brushing a project** pleases the development crew and annoys the stewards, **a first ascent** credits both ends of an axis only if the style was good, and on the odd-jobs board **the best-paying gig is the one that costs you most** (shooting guidebook photos, $130, against the old guard and the stewards both) while trail work buys it back. Push the stewards far enough and **access gets pulled** — the crag shuts for nine days, which is a season rather than a life sentence. Standing drifts toward nothing over months: the scene remembers, but not forever. The town is authored data — six venues with hours, prices and one service each, where **the hours are the mechanic**: the diner shuts at nine so a long day means eating from a warmer, and the gear shop keeps banker's hours so a resole competes with the window.
- 2026-08-18 — **Seasons: the window migrates, and a job costs you the winter** (`SeasonalCentreF`, `SeasonName`). One `baseTempF` covered the whole year, so windows sat in the same part of the day forever — which is why a nine-to-five and an east-facing crag never conflicted. The year now swings 20°F either side of 48: **summer centres on 68°F where only dawn is cool enough and just 33% of days are worth walking to the crag for; winter centres on 28°F where the good hours are 14:12** — the middle of the working day. Measured, **44% of winter windows are unreachable on a workday** against 0% in summer, and across a year the salaried climber arrives too late for about twice as many windows (60 vs 34, 48 vs 26, 45 vs 22). Written up with its limits: sends fall (7→2 on one seed) but not catastrophically, because weekends and the shoulder seasons hand most of the year back, and money is still solved completely. The trap is real, moderate, and seasonal. One probe bug found on the way: it was climbing in the window's *peak* conditions even when the clock was hours past the window, which hid the entire effect.
- 2026-08-18 — **The odd-jobs board and the salaried job — and the trap is not a trap yet** (`Sim/DirtbagJobs.*`, `notes/phase3-jobs.md`). Ten authored gigs, three a day, pay wobbling ±15%, and the coupling worth having: **some need the van**, so a breakdown costs the repair *and* the work that would have paid for it. Odd-job pay goes to debt first like any other money. The salaried job solves money exactly as designed — a salaried year ends on **$19,669** against the dirtbag's **$145** — and then **fails to cost anything**: the salaried climber climbs the *same number of days*, because work ends at 17:00 and Roadside Crag is east-facing, so its window is the evening. A nine-to-five and an east-facing crag simply do not conflict. Raising the energy cost gives 3/4/4 sends on one seed and 6/2/2 on another, which is noise, so the dial is back where it started rather than tuned until a number looked right. Written up as measured: the trap needs conditions that *move* — aspect (a west-facing crag would be gutted by a nine-to-five), seasons (one `baseTempF` keeps windows in the same part of the day forever), or a reason spontaneity pays. Seasons are the cheapest fix and the one to do next.
- 2026-08-18 — **Phase 3 reaches the player, and bills you cannot pay become debt** (**SAVE_VERSION 7**, `notes/phase3-placement.md`). Cash used to go negative and sit there with nothing behind it; it now floors at zero, the shortfall waits as `owed`, and the next wage clears it before reaching your pocket — a hole with something in it at last. Engine side: mirrors for shoes and all six van parts, `Dirtbag|Gear` and `Dirtbag|Van` nodes, and **every Travel spot now actually drives** — hours on the clock, hours on the van, and the chance that the part you have been ignoring picks this morning. A broken van refuses to go anywhere, which is the only "no" in the game, and the way out is four hours with a spanner rather than money. Two spots to place: **Kind = Van** (does the best repair you can afford — replace, patch, or bodge) and **Kind = GearShop** (resole while the uppers hold, $165 when they will not). The HUD carries what you owe and what is falling apart.
- 2026-08-18 — **The van: wear, breakdowns, and the lump the economy was missing** (`Sim/DirtbagVan.*`, **SAVE_VERSION 6**). Six parts wearing by the hour driven, so the crag you drive to costs more than the one you walk to. Nothing fails without warning — a part must be past its life first, and the van says so (*"the belt is on borrowed time"*). Three ways out: **bodge free for four hours, patch for cash twice, replace properly** — and bodging is always available, which is load-bearing: a stranded player with no money can always spend a morning under the van, so **breakdowns take your season and never your save** (pinned by test). The radiator wears 2.5× faster on warm days, so hot afternoons are both when you cannot climb and when the drive home is a gamble. Tuning took three passes: the first numbers gave 16 breakdowns a year (a running gag, not a van), 4.5× longer lives gave 1 (too rare to notice), and the settled ones give 2–3 in year one. Then it **aged on its own** — across three years the rate climbs to ~9/year because a patch returns only 60% and parts take two before needing replacement, with nearly half of all later repairs bodged because the money was not there. Cash bottoms at $0 with a day too broke to eat. That is the shape the gate wanted: not a bigger drip, but a bill on a morning you had plans.
- 2026-08-18 — **Phase 3 opens: gear that wears, and an honest look at the money loop** (`Sim/DirtbagGear.*`, `notes/phase3-economy.md`). Measured first: a year earned $4,860 across 81 shifts against $5,150 of costs, cash never below $93, **22% of days worked**, and no decision all year forced by money — because bills are a flat drip with nothing to do with climbing, so working more always solves them and the two loops never meet. Shoes are the first cost that scales with what you actually do: rubber wears **by the move** and faster the harder you pull, dead rubber costs grades before it costs money (1.1 at zero, squared, worst on edging holds, so a worn pair pushes you onto slopers long before it stops you), and the decision is resole at $55 while the uppers hold or $165 when they will not. Cash now bottoms at $11 — but the notes say plainly that most of that is a probe fix (the player had never eaten once all year, hunger topping out at 30 against a threshold of 45) and shoes are only 6% of the year's spend. What shoes bought is the *shape* of the cost, not its size. The economy still has no lump, which is what van breakdowns are for, and that is the next bite.
- 2026-08-18 — **Freshness is worth something, and the session says where you are.** Skin bit only below 3, only on crimps, and capped at 0.45 grades — so skin 9 and skin 3 were identical to climb on and a season played fresh matched one played wrecked. It is now a curve across the whole range (squared: 9→7 is nearly free, 3→1 decides everything) with holds mattering, so a V5 on V5 crimps runs 51% fresh against 7% on shot tips while jugs only fall 78%→58%. A jug day on wrecked tips is now a real option rather than a free one. Re-measured across three seeds, resting gives **~2.6× the sends** and no seed does worse. Paired with `ReadSession` (*Ready / Cold / SkinThin / Wrecked*), which the wall says when you walk up mid-session — the warmth trap was that a line you cannot start is one you can never warm up on, and nothing said so; a test pins that two or three easy problems clear it. The season now reads like a season: nine lines tried instead of one, first-move odds 52% against 4%, and *Send Train* sitting at 139 burns with a high point of two moves.
- 2026-08-18 — **Played a season headless, and found that flailing was the fastest way to get strong** (`Sim/tools/season.cpp`, `notes/phase2-season-probe.md`). Training read the grade on the tag and never `result.highpoint`, so falling off move one of something impossible trained exactly as well as nearly doing it — a policy that always picked the hardest line reached **V8.7 in a year without a single send**. Targeted skills now scale by how far up you actually got, with a floor for what pulling on teaches anyway; the same year runs V5.0 → V5.9 and a test pins it. Two findings recorded and deliberately not rushed into fixes: **warmth is earned per move, so a line you cannot start is one you can never warm up on** (self-reinforcing, and the game never says so), and **rest days buy almost nothing** because skin is conserved — ~550 burns a year however you spread them. That second one first appeared as a clean 3× improvement on one seed and evaporated across five; it is written up as disproved rather than quietly dropped. A year otherwise runs clean: no drift, no broke days, the Lot takes lines on schedule, and the save round-trips at the end.
- 2026-08-18 — **The dog** (`Sim/DirtbagDog.*`, **SAVE_VERSION 5**), and with it Phase 2's named scope is complete. A stray at the Lot that stops being one after three or four meals — no prompt, no adoption screen, because the life does not provide one. It costs about $1.50 a day, is worth a little psyche when settled and rather more against you when hungry, and lands in `ClimberForSession` beside fatigue so it is in the body you climb in rather than a line of text. The sharp part is the van: it runs ~18F above air, the conditions system already knows the air temperature hour by hour, and above 78F a van is not somewhere to leave a dog. A shift is four hours at a desk it cannot come to — measured over 600 days, a 9am shift is safe every time and a 3pm one is not, two days in three. **So the dog changes when you work, not whether**, and it never blocks you: it tells you afterwards ("You could hear it from the desk. The van hit 86F.") and takes a fifth to three quarters of a grade of psyche. Harness 26,016 → 26,070; v1 through v4 saves all still load.
- 2026-08-18 — **The Lot reaches the player** (`notes/phase2-lot.md`): a Day Spot with **Kind = Fire** — rest with company, naming who is there and what they are on this week — plus **B at any crag wall** to ask for beta, answered by whoever can actually help rather than whoever you know best (Trish is delighted to help and cannot). Rapport is earned by turning up and climbing, spent on beta and psyche. Sleep resolves the Lot's day before yours ends, so a line lost is news you wake to, in orange on the HUD: *"Dev got the arete left of Diesel."* Mirrors for partners and bonds, and the bond list added to the `FDirtbagPlayerState` converters — without that the save round-trip through the engine would have dropped every friendship silently on the way past.
- 2026-08-18 — **The Lot: five named people, three with careers of their own** (`Sim/DirtbagPartner.*`, **SAVE_VERSION 4**). Margo (V5, twenty seasons, undersells every line), Dev (V8, "will have your project if you leave it lying around"), Trish (V3, terrible beta delivered with total confidence), plus Ray and Bo, who do not climb — the people who stopped are the ones who remember why the lines are called what they are. They climb whether the player does or not, which means **the open lines are not reserved for you**: measured over a season with no player, the Lot takes nothing in the first 30 days, both moderates by day 90, four of five within a year, and the V10 blank wall not for three. A month's grace, then real pressure. Rapport grows by turning up and fades by not; beta shared scales with it (a stranger tells you where the crux is, a friend gives you most of what they know); company is worth psyche and deliberately never worth a grade. Almost none of it is saved — strength is a pure function of seed and date, so only rapport and who-got-there-first are career state, and a test asserts the derived half never reaches the file. The Lot rolls on its own named stream: a test plays the same day with and without it and pins the attempt traces identical, because who else was at the crag must never change how your burns resolve. Harness 19,872 → 26,016.
- 2026-08-18 — **A first ascent a new player can actually reach.** The gate says "names their own first ascent inside 6 hours" and the three open projects were V7, V8 and V10 against a starting V5 climber — measured, the easiest took a *V6* climber three days, so the point of the whole phase sat behind a season of training. Unclimbed is not a synonym for hard: two moderate lines join the book, open because the landing is a boulder field and because the rock looks loose, not because anyone found them difficult. A new player now reads *the slab right of the pull-off* as a warmup and *the short wall behind the cattle grid* (book V5, really V4) as at their limit, and takes either in about two in-game days after two hours with a brush. The hard end is untouched and still refuses them. Also: **rest is a day verb** (`Rest`), and a Day Spot with Kind = Rest waits exactly until the window opens — the window was the centrepiece of the phase and nothing could pass time on purpose, so waiting for the shade line was advice rather than a move.
- 2026-08-18 — First-ascent pipeline reaches the player: **C to clean** at any outdoor wall (half an hour on the brush per press, costing the day's time and energy), and a send on an unclimbed line raises a naming prompt. The prompt is a flag plus a HUD line rather than a widget class in C++, so what it looks like stays entirely in the editor and the moment is never invisible if the widget is missing. `notes/phase2-naming-widget.md` is the click-level build for the one thing that genuinely needs UMG — a text box — including the input-mode step that otherwise leaves you able to see the box but not type in it. Walking away from the prompt costs nothing: the ascent happened, and the line stays yours to name.
- 2026-08-18 — **The first-ascent pipeline: clean → work → send → name** (`Sim/DirtbagFirstAscent.*`, **SAVE_VERSION 3**). Phase 2's headline gate, and deliberately not a quest — there is no lock to pick and nothing grants permission. A virgin line is simply filthy, and filthy rock climbs ~4 grades harder (`dirtGradePenalty`) than it will after an afternoon with a brush: a V6 climber sends a clean V5 over half the time and a filthy one under 5%. You may throw yourself at it dirty and fail, which is the honest version of a gate. Cleaning costs ~1.75h to reach workable and composes with the window — brush through the greasy middle of the day, climb when the rock comes into condition. The payoff is what no other route can give: **a project's true grade is hidden and differs from the book's guess** (the three lines here go V7/V7, V8/V7, V9/**V10**), so doing it is the first time anybody knows, and the naming is yours. `NameFirstAscent` never touches `route.name` — the ledger key — so a career's burns survive the renaming. Measured: a V6 climber takes 3 days and 14 burns on the arete but 138 days and 699 on the same-grade traverse (shape beats nominal grade), and the blank wall at V10 refuses even a V9 — the crag keeps one line to grow into. Engine side: `Dirtbag|FirstAscent` nodes, wall prompts leading with the dirt, and project ledgers seeded when the crag loads rather than on first touch — every attempt path invents a *clean* ledger through `MemoryFor` if one is missing, so seeding late would have made the entire mechanic silently absent. Harness 19,613 → 19,813, clean under ASan/UBSan.
- 2026-08-18 — **Roadside Crag: the guidebook as data** (`Sim/DirtbagCrag.*`). 25 named lines and 3 open projects, authored rather than generated — a gym board is a clean V0–V15 ladder that resets, a crag is a fixed book where grades cluster where the rock happened to be climbable, sandbags are famous and specific (*Second Breakfast* has said V2 for years and climbs V3), and some lines have never gone at all. Stars run across the range instead of tracking it, so *Shade Line* V4 is a three-star classic and *Sloper Roulette* V5 is filler. Measured against the ladder before shipping: a V5 climber reads 11 warmups, 4 comfortable, **7 at their limit**, 3 projects and 3 refusals — a day with shape — while a V7 has exhausted the place, which is the pressure to take the projects. `CragLine.displayName` exists from the first commit so naming a first ascent never moves `route.name`, which is the ledger key ProjectMemory and the save file record; renaming a line out from under a career would orphan every burn already put into it, and a test pins it. Engine side: mirrors, `Dirtbag|Crag` nodes, and one board index serving two venues, so moving a wall from gym to crag is a flag rather than a rebuild. The crag owns its own aspect and sets it on load — a second copy is how rock ends up climbing in one aspect's shade with its window computed for another. Harness 19,111 → 19,613 checks.
- 2026-08-18 — **Conditions and the prime window** (`Sim/DirtbagConditions.*`, `notes/phase2-window.md`). The resolver has eaten a `friction` scalar since Phase 0 and nothing ever produced one; this produces it. Seed + day → weather on its own rng stream; weather + aspect + hour → *rock* temperature, which banks the sun's heat and sheds it on a lag, so prime is the crossing when the rock cools back through ideal — the reason you wait after the shade line passes. Three things were measured before anything was written: `frictionWeight` needed no change (0.6 grades reads as nothing and swings a limit V5 from 39% to 70%); the window's **length** is the whole design (skin allows 8–12 burns, so a window over two hours holds a whole day's skin, waiting becomes free, and a limit project falls 98% of the time to someone who knows nothing about it); and getting the peak off the boundaries took three models before dawn-pinning fell from 50% to 12%. Gate passed and pinned by test: 27% with no recon, 62% with six, 60% once the skin is gone before the window opens. Aspect decides *when* — east-facing is bimodal, west peaks in the morning, south comes good at 6pm, north tracks the air. Harness 7,631 → 19,111 checks. Engine side: mirrors, six `Dirtbag|Conditions` nodes, HUD line, and both attempt paths finally resolving against real friction instead of the 0.5 default. The gym stays indoors and flat on purpose.
- 2026-08-18 — Restored `ADirtbagClimbWall::FinishAttempt` and `EndSession`, deleted by an anchor-slicing edit in the HUD commit; the linker named only the first because the sole caller of the second went with it. `EndSession` now also clears `FDirtbagSessionReadout`, which nothing did — the session panel would have stayed pinned to the screen with a dead route's numbers from the first attempt onward. Added `tools/check-engine-defs.py`: every declaration in a Dirtbag header must have a definition qualified by its declaring class, every `Sim/*.cpp` must have a bridge file. Both are the real build failures of this phase, and both are verified against them — brace-balance cannot catch a deleted function, and a bare-name search for `FinishAttempt` is satisfied by the sim's own.
- 2026-08-17 — **SAVE_VERSION 2 and the first real migration.** Project ledgers now record the route's guidebook grade on first touch, so a career survives the gym resetting its walls; v1 saves migrate to grade −1 (unknown, never guessed) and a hand-written v1 file is pinned as a test. `SummarizeCareer` / `CareerLine` read a career back out of the ledgers alone — ability, hardest send, open projects, and the nemesis line you've fed the most burns.
- 2026-08-17 — Reading the line from the ground (`ReadRoute` → Warmup / Comfortable / At your limit / Project / Not this year, judged against the *guidebook* grade so a sandbag reads honest until you're on it) and fatigue that actually arrives: energy now scales with how far the line is above you, and its penalty fades in from `freshEnergy` instead of waiting at a threshold no session ever reached. A limit session now has an arc — burn twelve is worse than burn two, on thin skin and low nerve. Measured before building: a day clock was considered and dropped, since skin ends a session at burn 8 while the clock reads 9am.
- 2026-08-17 — **Balance: the skill ladder recalibrated** (`concepts/BALANCE-SKILL-LADDER.md`). Simulating a month exposed that `skill/100*18` made a default 50-stat climber a V9 — nothing on a V0–V7 board resisted anyone (45-stat climbers sent V7 74% of the time). New `SkillToGrade` dial (span 14, floor −2) makes skill 50 a V5 climber, shared by resolver and trainer instead of duplicated. Training retuned to match (challenge gate now functional, diminishing returns, endurance no longer 50× faster than everything else). Two tests recalibrated, `TestGradesResist` added so the ladder can't silently re-flatten. Money loop deliberately untouched — that's Phase 3's gate against the 2D reference.
- 2026-08-17 — Central game state: `UDirtbagGameInstance` (registered in DefaultEngine.ini) owns seed/player/day, loads on boot, saves on sleep, serves the cached gym board and per-day session seeds. Climb wall retrofitted: with the game instance present it carries board problem #BoardIndex and commits attempts day-integrated (standalone fallback kept for test maps); session HUD gains a DAY/$/ENERGY row. New `ADirtbagSleepSpot`: walk up, E, day ends, save writes.
- 2026-08-17 — Day loop + save reach Blueprint: `FDirtbagPlayerState`/`FDirtbagDayState` mirrors, day-verb nodes (Wake Up, Pass Hours, Eat Meal, Work Shift, Start Gym Session, Sleep To Next Day, Gym Board), day-integrated attempt paths (Day Attempt for replay, Begin Day Live Attempt / Commit Live Attempt for HOLD TO CLIMB), and Save/Load To File under Saved/SaveGames — serialization stays in Sim/, nodes only move bytes.
- 2026-08-17 — Phase 1 groundwork: day-loop sim core (`Sim/DirtbagDay.*` — day clock, hunger/energy/cash, meals and shifts, weekly bills, overnight skin regen and psyche drift, training creep from hard attempts, deterministic gym route board) and the first save file (`Sim/DirtbagSave.*` — versioned key=value serialization of career state, exact double round-trip, future-version refusal, migration registry live and tested from v1). Harness grows twelve matching tests including a 7-day integration loop.
- 2026-08-17 — **Phase 0 — Session Proof: DONE.** All four gates passed on the blockout wall (stat-block, tension, watcher, deterministic sim). The session-model pivot is validated. CURRENT MILESTONE advances to Phase 1 — Gym Slice.
- 2026-08-17 — HOLD TO CLIMB goes interactive: ADirtbagClimbWall gains a live mode driven by the live-attempt API — hold Space to charge the grip meter, release in the sweet window to latch (execution from timing), release early to shake out, over-grip and the move fires itself badly. Debug HUD (pump / grip / best-case odds), mount + top-out anims, sim-side commit of live attempts. Verb dials on the actor; replay mode kept as a toggle.
- 2026-08-15 — Engine wrapper layer (`Source/DirtbagUE/DirtbagSimTypes.*`, `DirtbagSimLibrary.*`): USTRUCT/UENUM mirrors and Blueprint nodes over route gen, the session loop, and the live attempt. Sim-side, `AttemptInSession` decomposed into `DeriveAttemptRng`/`BuildSessionAttemptInput`/`CommitAttempt` so live attempts commit through the same accounting (equivalence pinned by test). SETUP.md §4 rewritten as a Blueprint-level checklist.
- 2026-08-15 — Live attempt API (`BeginAttempt`/`PeekOdds`/`StepMove`/`ShakeOut`/`FinishAttempt`): the resolver unrolled so HOLD TO CLIMB can drive move-by-move, with release-to-shake as a sim-arbitrated verb (diminishing returns + hang tax). `ResolveAttempt` reimplemented as a bot on the live core — equivalence pinned by test. Deliberate delta: `peakPump` now records the true pre-recovery peak.
- 2026-08-15 — Session loop layered over the resolver (`Sim/DirtbagSessionLoop.*`): warmup → attempts → skin budget within a day, plus `ProjectMemory` across days (highpoint ledger, beta learned from touched moves, first-send style). Resolver gains cold-start and psyche terms, neutral by default — existing outcomes and golden vectors untouched. Harness grows six matching tests.
- 2026-08-15 — Project decided: Dirtbag reimagining, session-model pivot ("2D minigames, 3D staging"). Repo scaffolded: concepts, roadmap, `Sim/` core (RNG + types + session resolver) with standalone test harness, `SETUP.md`.
